市場焦點
美國
• 9月CPI年增3.7%、PPI年增6.3%，雙雙高於前期 ，能源價格的急升是通膨回升的主要推力 。
雖然核心CPI年增率僅升至2.6%，但住房與服務價格仍維持黏著，使通膨降溫過程出現反覆；
同時PPI增幅明顯高於 CPI，反映企業成本壓力偏高 ，未來不排除進一步向終端價格傳導 。整
體而言，CPI與PPI同步回升反映通膨降溫進程仍存在不確定性 ，進而令市場重估通膨回落速
度與對Fed貨幣政策路徑的審慎預期。
AI
• AI雲端業者 CoreWeave的5年期CDS利差一度突破 1,140個基點 ，旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近
15%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延至股票市場 。全球最大半導體設備商ASML表示，客戶已開始洽
談2029至2030年的訂單；晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期 。與此同時，台灣出口增

速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季
相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。
中國
• 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力
仍在，彭博表示以GDP平減指數衡量 ，經歷三年下滑後 ，整體經濟預計將在 2026年走出通縮 ，
投資上短期可聚焦，受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。

美股獲利引擎轉向半導體/核心AI與能源
• 雖然標普500指數在2026年第三季展現出獲利韌性 ，但隨著油價上漲侵蝕利潤率 、消費者受通膨壓抑 ，以及分析師下調未
來預測動能，市場未來的容錯空間正在縮小。
• AI與半導體仍為獲利領頭羊：科技與半導體持續為標普500指數獲利成長的核心動力，其中約20家企業組成的核心AI一籃子
股票(權重約34.6%)對指數獲利的貢獻度已超過科技七雄(Mag 7)，且此優勢預計將至少延續至2028年底。
• 油價推升能源產業，接棒成為強棒：受到近期國際油價上揚影響，能源產業獲利預測顯著上修，預計獲利成長勁道將延續至
2027年初。在接下來的三個季度中，能源板塊的收益成長表現預計將超越科技七雄。
• 消費與運輸業面臨成本壓頂：相較於科技與能源的強勁，必需消費品與交通運輸業正承受顯著壓力。例如，達美航空受高燃

料成本拖累，而Nike與百事可樂則反映出消費者因持續通膨而縮減支出的困境。
7月以來EPS和營收成長預期修正
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
剔除能源、科技七雄和AI一籃子股票後的指數收益成長

顧
利潤率高檔盤整、關稅退款帶來短補，但動能已見放緩
• 利潤率高檔盤整，淨利潤率提升受限：標普500成分股第二季營運利潤率(18.7%)與淨利潤率(15.6%)均創下新高。雖然第三
季營運利潤率預估可進一步提升至19.4%，但淨利潤率預計微幅上升至15.8%，顯示整體企業淨利潤的擴張動能正漸趨平緩 。
• 700億美元關稅退款提供短線補藥： 受惠於美國政府依據國際緊急經濟權力法 (IEEPA)的追償退稅，估計仍有至少700億美元
的關稅款將退還給標普 500成分股(主要集中於工業、非必需消費及醫療保健等 )，這預計能為2026年標普500指數每股收益
(EPS)成長帶來約235個基點、利潤率提升約34個基點的短期提振。
• 上修動能開始轉弱：雖然整體財報指引動量仍維持正值，但過去六週內，分析師對未來12個月EPS與收入的修正動能出現頻

繁下修，這意味著市場先前的極度樂觀情緒正在消退，未來財報季度要優於預期的門檻正在提高。
• 小結與投資建議： 標普500指數短期內靠著半導體 /AI與能源雙引擎拉抬 ，加上關稅退款的一次性利多注入 ，下半年整體獲
利依然具備支撐。然而，高油價帶來的營運成本上升 ，以及終端消費力道的疲軟 ，正逐漸侵蝕非科技/非能源企業的利潤 ，
而大盤廣度不足(僅少數板塊支撐獲利)，代表未來投資風險正逐漸集中 ，但AI算力需求仍屬硬性需求，但波動加劇下，投資
建議以定期定額方式投資美股核心AI與半導體龍頭股票。
利潤率預測
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
各產業第二季度收到的關稅退款
未來12月EPS預期修正動能與營收

顧
美國9月CPI、PPI 同步升溫，能源衝擊擴大通膨與利率風險
• 能源價格急升，消費通膨再升溫：美國9月CPI月增0.6%、
年增3.7%，高於前月 3.4%，其中汽油月增 9.1%、能源
價格月增 5.2%，汽油單項即貢獻整體 CPI當月漲幅逾半；
另一方面，核心CPI月增0.3%、年增2.6%、住房指數月
增0.4%，顯示能源雖是本次通膨回升主因 ，但住房及部
分服務價格仍具黏著性，令通膨降溫進程面臨反覆。
• 上游價格大幅攀升 ，成本傳導風險升高： 9月PPI最終需
求月增1.3%、年增6.3%，創2022年3月以來最大增幅，
其中最終需求商品月增 2.6%，能源價格大漲 10.8%，汽
油價格更跳升 20.3%；核心PPI月增0.5%、年增5.0%，
顯示價格壓力不僅來自能源 ，也涉及部分商品與服務環

節；後續是否進一步推升至 CPI，仍須觀察企業成本轉
嫁程度及需求變化。
• 市場看法： 通膨回升恐提高 Fed再度升息機率 ，並延長
高利率維持時間 ，甚至不排除進一步緊縮的可能 ，短期
有利支撐美債殖利率及美元表現 ，不過股、債市後續走
勢仍將受到貨幣政策預期與風險情緒變化影響 。資產配
置建議可關注具成本轉嫁能力 、現金流穩健的能源類股；
債券則宜以短中天期 、高信用評等標的為主 ，以降低利
率敏感度。黃金可作為分散地緣政治及通膨風險的輔助
配置，但不宜將任何單一資產視為必然受惠標的。
CPI 與核心 CPI 走勢變動

顧
供給面推動中國9月CPI升0.9%
• 供給面推動中國9月通膨復甦：中國9月通膨數據顯示，CPI年增率由8月的0.8%升至0.9%，連續兩個月擴大，但低於市場預期
的1.0%，物價上漲主要受到能源及旅遊價格帶動，而食品價格跌幅進一步擴大，使整體通膨壓力仍相對溫和，扣除食品與能源
後，核心CPI維持在1.0%，反映內需需求尚未明顯升溫 。另一方面，PPI年增率由3.8%大幅升至5.0%，優於市場預期，且月增
率達1.2%，創4月以來最大漲幅，顯示上游原物料及工業品價格回升，須關注未來是否逐步傳導至消費端價格。
• 展望後市： 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求 ，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力仍 在，彭博表示 以
GDP平減指數衡量，經歷三年下滑後，整體經濟預計將在2026年走出通縮，值得注意的是，這種供給面驅動的通膨復甦，將降

低官方推出需求刺激措施的緊迫感 ，而要反轉通縮循環正需要官方舉措，投資上短期可聚焦受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的
半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.14
通膨集中於能源和AI相關行業

顧
過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數
的兩倍
• 過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數的兩倍：主因為獲利帶動成長，整體而言，新興市場企
業獲利表現強勁 ，而且仍持續加速成長 ，高盛預估2026年新興市場企業盈餘成長率將達 55%，超過標普500企業盈餘成長速
度的兩倍，且新興市場股票估值較美國股市存在超過40%的折價空間，評價相對具吸引力，今年以來流入新興市場股市的資金
已接近500億美元，創下過去20年來最強勁紀錄之一。更重要的是新興市場國家目前貢獻了全球近 80%的AI硬體出口。隨著AI
產業持續發展，受惠範圍有望從少數龍頭企業擴大至更多國家及公司，特別是那些能夠協助解決供應鏈瓶頸的企業。
• 結論：後市來看在AI產業趨勢、相對低估的評價水準，以及強勁企業獲利成長三大因素支撐下，新興市場股票未來一年仍具備

進一步上漲的潛力，有機會延續目前的強勢表現。

AI投資支撐美國成長，融資需求升高使長端利率與信用風險並存
• AI基礎建設持續擴張 ，成為企業投資重要動能 ：大型雲端業者
持續投入資料中心 、半導體、網路設備與電力基礎設施 ，帶動
相關產業資本支出與供應鏈需求 。摩根大通資產管理指出 ，納
入資料中心開發商及半導體投資後，AI 基礎建設至 2030 年的
累計投資需求可能達約 5兆美元 ，顯示AI投資循環仍具規模 ，
但實際經濟貢獻與企業獲利 ，仍取決於投資效率及商業化成果 。
• 外部融資需求增加 ，債券供給與投資回報成關鍵： 隨AI投資規
模擴大，大型科技企業逐步增加公司債與外部融資 ，在企業債
與公債供給同步增加下 ，長天期利率面臨上行壓力 。若未來AI
獲利成長未能跟上資本支出擴張速度 ，可能壓縮自由現金流與

資本報酬率，進一步擴大信用利差 ，並提高高估值科技股的評
價修正風險
• 市場觀察：AI基礎建設仍是未來數年推動全球科技投資與企業
數位轉型的成長主軸，相關需求具備中長期發展空間，而2026
年市場已逐漸轉向檢視投資回報率 、現金流創造能力及融資結
構是否具備可持續性 ，因此單純依賴資本支出擴張已不足以支
撐所有AI概念股評價 。建議配置可兼顧具獲利與現金流支撐的
AI供應鏈，以及高信用評等 、中短天期債券；對長天期債券則
須評估供給與利率風險 ，並審慎檢視高負債 AI企業的償債能力
及投資回報。
資料來源：Goldman Sachs，永豐金證券， 2026.10.12

AI熱潮、高債務與網路威脅，IMF示警三大金融穩定風險升高
資料來源：Bloomberg Finance L.P， Dealogic，Zero day Clock and IMF staff calculations.， 2026.10.13
AI相關風險持續升溫 主權債券殖利率攀升 網路資安威脅升高
大型雲端科技企業債券發行規模
（占投資等級公司債市場比重) 10年期公債殖利率平均變動幅度 零時差漏洞占比（%）
• AI估值高度集中，市場重定價風險升溫：IMF於10月《全球金融穩定報告》指出，AI相關投資已成為全球成長的重要支撐，
但資金與漲勢高度集中於少數企業及市場，一旦獲利成長不如預期或估值遭重新檢視，可能引發科技股修正並透過風險偏好
下降擴散至整體金融市場。
• 通膨與財政疑慮恐推升債券市場波動： IMF認為，若能源價格推升通膨壓力或市場對財政永續性產生疑慮 ，主權債殖利率可

能進一步上升，進而提高全球融資成本與資產重估風險；在高利率與高債務環境下，金融條件快速緊縮的情況將明顯提高。
• 網路安全風險成為潛在威脅： 隨金融體系數位化程度提高 ，IMF將大型網路攻擊列為重要風險來源之一 。若攻擊事件波及支
付系統、金融基礎設施或雲端服務平台，不僅可能影響市場運作效率，也可能透過信心衝擊與流動性壓力擴大金融市場波動。
• 投資建議：市場成長動能仍受AI支撐，但在金融穩定風險上升情境下，建議採取「AI受惠股＋高品質債券＋防禦型資產」的
槓鈴策略；核心配置可聚焦全球AI基礎建設供應鏈及投資等級債，藉由企業長期獲利成長與債券避險功能，同時因應估值修
正及金融條件收緊風險。

AI隱憂浮現但動能仍在
• CoreWeave信用風險亮紅燈：市場憂心雲端業者在算力租賃業務產生足夠回報前，恐難以負擔龐大債務，導致信用風險升
溫。上週，AI雲端業者CoreWeave的5年期信用違約交換（CDS）利差一度突破1,140個基點，升至通常反映嚴重信用壓力
的水準；旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近15%，其中2032年到期的無擔保債券於上週三跌破面值的80%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延
至股票市場。
• AI半導體產業的基本面仍具韌性：全球最大半導體設備商艾司摩爾（ASML）表示，客戶已開始洽談2029至2030年的訂單；
晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期。與此同時，台灣出口增速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯
示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。

美銀美林調查指出，全球經理人對景氣與股市仍維持偏多立場
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10
• 經理人樂觀情緒維持高檔： 美銀美林10月全球基金經理人調查顯示 ，市場風險偏好仍高 ，平均現金水位維持在 3.9%的低檔，
科技股配置則升至2020年8月以來最高，反映經理人對AI產業及企業獲利前景仍具信心 。然而，整體經濟成長預期已轉趨保
守，淨4%的受訪者預期未來 12個月經濟成長放緩 。極端風險方面，債券殖利率無序攀升仍居首位 ，占比31%；AI泡沫及通
膨再起分別為23%及20%。此外，39%的經理人認為，大型雲端業者資本支出是系統性信用事件的主要潛在來源 ，顯示市場
焦點已從AI需求成長延伸至龐大資本支出的獲利回收能力。

• 美國公債殖利率走高 ，債券的收益吸引力逐漸回升： 調查顯示，37%的受訪者表示，當美國10年期公債殖利率升至 6%時，
才會考慮由股票轉向債券；另有19%認為目前殖利率已具吸引力，顯示部分經理人已開始重新評估固定收益資產的配置價值 。
不過，債券整體仍處淨減碼，且經理人對科技股的配置持續升高 ，反映市場尚未全面轉向防禦。後續仍須關注美債殖利率變
化、AI資本支出的投資回報，以及企業獲利能否支撐偏高的市場評價。
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10

台積電財報佳卻未激勵股價，短線面臨總經干擾
• 台積電Q3營收、獲利及EPS同步改寫單季新高：營收年增50.9%、季增17.6%，稅後純益7,630.6億元，年增68.7％、季增8
％；每股稅後純益(EPS)29.43元，整體優於市場預期，但毛利率66.4％較第二季的67.7％下降。
• 財測展望：第四季財測營收預估 523~543億美元，季增上看15％、年增65％，毛利率預估65%~67%，反映高階晶片需求
依然強勁。全年展望方面，預期2026年美元營收年增約44%，較上季預估的「略高於40%」說法更加明確。此外，重申全
年資本支出600~640億美元不變。
• 市場反應：台積電法說會隔日股價小幅下跌，法說會未出現激勵行情，主要原因包含美國通膨升溫壓力、外資連續調節部位，

以及毛利率略為下降。短線籌碼面可能仍會調整，但台積電董事長魏哲家在法說會中強調，第二波AI需求正強勁興起，台積
電正透過多種途徑全力擴充產能，顯示高階晶片前景明確，中長期具投資價值，多家投資機構也紛紛上調目標價。
資料來源：台積電法說會，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.15。
法說會重點整理 說明
AI需求持續升溫
魏哲家坦言，目前仍難以判斷晶圓供需缺口將擴大或縮小，因為
客戶需求持續增加，台積電也正積極提高產能。儘管消費性電子
市場仍受到零組件漲價及總體經濟不確定性影響，但先進製程需
求依然強勁，公司預估2026年全年美元營收將年增約44％。
擴廠進度
(1)台南科學園區新建3奈米廠預計2027年下半年量產，並決定在
台南再增建一座3奈米廠，規畫2028年投入量產。
(2)美國亞利桑那州第二座晶圓廠已完成廠房興建，預計2027年
第三季量產，時程較原先規畫略為提前；日本第二座晶圓廠則將
導入3奈米製程，預計2028年量產。
台積電證實洽談Terafab
合作
魏哲家強調，目前相關合作仍在討論階段，尚未有結論。不過，

無論未來採取何種合作模式，晶圓廠都必須由台積電負責營運。
電力供應疑慮
美國電力供應情況是公司評估AI長期需求的重要因素之一，因此
台積電進一步確認當地電力供應狀況，目前相關研究及評估結果
均良好，尚未發現明顯問題。

• 全球經濟正處於「韌性仍在、風險升高」的階段：國際貨幣基金組織(IMF)指出，AI投資熱潮持續推動美國、台灣等國家成
長，但中東地緣政治衝突、高通膨風險、政府債務問題與AI泡沫化疑慮，也同時為市場埋下不確定因素。
• 市場永遠充滿雜音，定期定額紀律投資更具優勢：本許多人認為定期定額的核心在於「平均成本」，但更重要的是幫助投資
人克服人性。市場上漲時持續投入，可以參與企業獲利成長帶來的成果，市場下跌時持續扣款，則能以較低價格累積更多單
位數。換句話說，定期定額讓投資人不需要每天判斷：明天會不會跌？現在是不是太貴？要不要先停扣觀望？長期而言，紀

律投資往往比短期擇時的成效更佳。
• AI時代更適合定期定額：儘管IMF認為人工智慧發展為全球經濟成長帶來貢獻，但同時也警告，若市場對AI未來獲利能力過
度樂觀，不排除出現股市估值修正。根據歷史經驗，每一次市場重大修正往往也孕育下一輪成長契機，不論AI發展是否出現
泡沫，善用定期定額策略可以在機會與風險之間達到平衡。
韌性仍在、風險升溫，紀律投資更穩健
任一月份定期定額進場投資試算
註：以MSCI全球指數2010/1/31~2025/12/31試算。假設定期定額每月月底投入、
原幣計算。指數資料試算僅供參考，非投資商品之實際績效。資料來源Bloomberg。
短進短出錯失行情的代價大
註：以美國標普500指數為試算範例，期間為1980年~2025年。資料來源Bloomberg。
投資人無法直接投資指數，資訊僅供歷史統計說明，非預測投資商品之績效。

3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

E Ink Holdings Inc.  | Asia Pacific
September  Sales +5% MoM/
+14% YoY
In this report, we focus on E Ink's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$3,613mn (+5% MoM/+14% YoY).
• 3Q26 sales came in at NT$9,936mn (-3% QoQ/-5% YoY), in line with our
estimate at NT$9,985mn (-2% QoQ/-4% YoY) and the consensus at NT
$9,774mn (-4% QoQ/-6% YoY).
Our view:
• E Ink's monthly sales run rate will need to stay at the September level

through year end to achieve its full-year revenue guidance of up 10-15% YoY
for 2026. This implies its 4Q26 revenue will be up by 9% QoQ, which still
looks within reach, despite the softer seasonality
• While the worst seems to be behind us and valuation is at a trough level of
12x 2027e P/E, we see no strong catalysts in the near term, despite our long-
term positive view on  e-paper demand across various applications. Stay EW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
E Ink Holdings Inc. (8069.TWO)
Base case, 12x 2027e P/E. We believe this valuation methodology best captures E Ink's earn-
ings growth momentum. We view our target multiple of 12x as fair, considering our estimated
17% earnings CAGR for 2025-28. The implied PEG of 0.7x is largely in line with its typical level
in 2022-25, when the stock was trading at an average P/E of 22x one-year forward consensus
EPS with a 37% earnings CAGR.

Risks to Upside
n Firmer-than-expected ESL orders
n Stronger demand for e-readers and e-notes
n Better macro conditions
n Milder competition
n Brisker inventory digestion
Risks to Downside
n Softer-than-expected ESL orders
n Slower demand for e-readers and e-notes
n Macro headwinds
n Intensified competition
n More protracted inventory digestion

Analyst Stock Ratings

12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Nanya 3Q beat; CXMT updates
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Charlie Chan

Lucas.Wang@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2875
Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
What’s Changed
Nanya Technology Corp.
(2408.TW) From To
Price Target NT$580.00 NT$600.00
DRAM supply constraints continue. Contract renewals drove
Nanya's 3Q beat.  CXMT's G5 milestone strengthens its tech
roadmap, but yield ramp and customer qualification take time.

Nanya 3Q beat; LTAs in spotlight:  Nanya reported 3Q revenue of NT$134bn, +62%
Q/Q, +612% Y/Y, 8% above consensus. 3Q gross profit was NT$114bn, +114% Q/Q,
12% above consensus, implying a GM of 85.7%. 3Q net profit was NT$88bn, +75%
Q/Q, 9% above consensus, implying a net margin of 65.1%. The 3Q Q/Q revenue
growth was driven mainly by ASP increase, which primarily reflects renewal of the
one-year and six-month L TAs, which were previously negotiated when ASPs were

lower; most  expired in 3Q26. Currently L TAs cover 50-70% of output; only 20% of
output is covered by three-year contracts; Nanya intends to extend the L TA
durations for longer-term supply according to customer demand, and there are
discussions on price floors and ceilings for the new contracts.
Nanya management sees continued DRAM shortage in 4Q and expects that ASP
may continue to improve: The supply constraint is the long lead time for new
capacity, and the undersupply is unlikely to be resolved in the near term. Nanya

targets capex  of NT$69.7bn in 2026 and NT$346bn  for the 35.9kwpm capacity of its
new Fab 5A from 2026-29. Management estimates that AI infra currently accounts
for ~20% of its revenue mix, but that could increase to 40-50% by 2029/30.
CXMT updates: On September 7 during Q&A, CXMT reiterated expectations for
tight DRAM supply in 2H26; its response on Apple expressed openness to global
customers without confirming a supply agreement. On September 21, after its G5

mass production announcement, CXMT clarified that G5 sales are not yet at scale,
while yield ramp-up and customer qualification would take time. Based on our
checks, CXMT's HBM3E yield is already 40%, and DRAM wafer input should ramp to
30kwpm by end-2027.
Constrained DRAM supply is the industry consensus: The 4QF26 earnings call for
Micron  (covered by Joseph Moore) highlighted that  persistent supply constraints are
expected through CY2027-28. It cited industry-wide clean room constraints owing to

lengthy construction; most of the increase in Micron's F27 capex (vs. its previous
plan) is allocated to construction, targeting clean room availability in late CY28 and
beyond. Micron also expects rising industry HBM wafer requirements to limit bit
growth, forecasting industry DRAM bit shipment growth of low-20s (%) in CY27-28.
Samsung (covered by Shawn Kim) also expects HBM to absorb nearly 30% of
industry DRAM wafer capacity in 2027, vs ~20% currently.

Stock implications:
• Nanya Tech (OW):  Price target raised to NT$600; 2026e, 2027e, and 2028e
EPS raised  22%, 18%, and 19%, respectively.
•  CXMT (OW): Base case value/price target unchanged at Rmb88.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Nanya Tech: Valuation Methodology
We raise our price target (base case scenario value) from NT$580 to NT$600: Our
price target reflects a BVPS multiple of 4.11x for 2026 (was 4.77x) versus ROE of 76% for
2026.  We lower our target multiple to reflect recent memory segment volatility. Our
2026e BVPS estimates is raised to NT$146.00 from NT$121.52. We expect DDR4 pricing
strength and L TAs to drive a re-rating.
We raise our bull and bear case values from NT$1,230 and NT$320 to NT$1,270 and NT

$330, respectively, implying 8.70x and 2.26x 2026e BVPS.

CXMT: Valuation Methodology
We keep our base case value/price target unchanged: We continue to apply RI
methodology to derive our price target. The price target did not change despite our
2026/27 earnings estimate increases because the contribution to calculated value from
our RI model comes mostly from the subsequent forecast period  and the continuing value.
Key RI assumptions remain unchanged, including cost of equity of 9.9%, , intermediate

growth rate of 11.0%, terminal growth rate of 3.0%, and cash dividend payout ratio of 51%,
.
Our base case value/price target now implies 14.3x 2027e EPS vs. our prior target multiple
of 18.4x 2027e BVPS, as we see a stronger DRAM market outlook given the continued
supply constraint; we also believe the G5 breakthrough reduces risks around CXMT's
technology roadmap.
Our bull case value remains unchanged at Rmb132; it now implies 21.5x our 2027e EPS.

We adjust our bear case value to Rmb40 from Rmb70 to account for recent volatility; it
now implies 6.5x 2027e EPS.

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

New capacity announcements do not overturn our bullish view.
AI demand and increasing substrate complexity should keep the
market undersupplied from 2027, supporting further pricing and
margin expansion. We raise earnings estimates and price targets
for Unimicron and NYPCB and reiterate OW on both.
More capacity, but limited near-term relief: Suppliers are stepping up investment,

but several newly announced projects are not scheduled to ramp until late 2028-30
or beyond. Our updated supply-demand model continues to indicate undersupply
from 2027 through 2030, with tighter supply in CY27-28, which should only be
more favorable to pricing. Therefore, capacity additions announced in the past few
months do not, in our view, diminish the magnitude of pricing gains over the next
two years.
CY27 pricing should accelerate, amid tighter supply-demand in CY27-28, but

visibility on the magnitude remains limited: We see ABF substrate prices rising
25%+ in CY27, below buy-side expectations of ~50%. Our supply/demand outlook
points to undersupply next year, with larger substrates, higher layer counts, and
greater manufacturing complexity further constraining usable output. Raw material
cost pass-through was a key driver of CY26 price increases, we expect supply
tightness to play a greater role in CY27. This should allow pricing to outpace cost

inflation, driving faster margin expansion. We remain constructive on pricing, but the
magnitude and timing of increases depends on customer-level negotiations.
With pricing a segment-wide tailwind, we prefer Unimicron for its stronger CY27
capacity growth: As supply tightens, the ability to deliver incremental volume
should become a key differentiator. We favor suppliers bringing capacity online in
CY27 over those whose expansion plans contribute in later years. Unimicron’s

Guangfu II ramp should add 10-15% to capacity in CY27, compared with 5-10%
growth at NYPCB through debottlenecking, giving Unimicron greater scope to
benefit from both pricing and volume growth. Unimicron is also our preferred play
heading into earnings, as more demanding expectations for NYPCB leave less room
for an upside surprise. But we remain OW on NYPCB given its networking and AMD
CPU exposure, alongside its proactive pricing strategy.
We reiterate Overweight on Unimicron and NYPCB: Reflecting stronger earnings

trajectories, we raise our EPS estimates for both companies. Based on upside to our
revised price targets, our order of preference is Unimicron (NT$1,775 PT; 24x CY28e
P/E; 41% upside), followed by NYPCB (NT$2,000 PT; 22x CY28e P/E; 39% upside).
Potential risks: 1) Weaker demand for PCs, general servers, and AI servers; 2)
meaningful capacity expansion plans; 3) T-glass shortage; 4) CoWoP displacing ABF
substrates.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
October 14, 2026 08:00 AM GMT

### Page 2

Idea
2
Supply/Demand Update: Undersupply Deepens in CY27–28
Our updated model points to a wider supply shortfall in CY27–28, despite additional
capacity: Incorporating the latest expansion plans and semiconductor demand forecasts,
we now expect ABF substrate demand to exceed available supply by 9.3% in CY27 and
13.7% in CY28, versus 6.2% and 9.4% previously. This represents a widening of 3.1ppt and
4.3ppt, respectively, relative to our prior estimates. We maintain our view that CY26 is a

broadly balanced market, followed by increasingly tight supply over the next two years.
The main demand upgrade comes from other ASICs, including Chinese AI chips: We
raise our shipment assumptions for this category to 6.4mn units in CY27 and 8.7mn in
CY28, from 4.6mn and 6.2mn previously. Our Nvidia GPU and server CPU forecasts remain
largely unchanged, while we lower AMD GPU estimates while also keeping CY27 Trainium
and TPU shipments largely unchanged. The tighter substrate outlook therefore reflects

stronger demand across a broader set of ASIC suppliers, rather than higher shipment
assumptions for every major AI platform. We also raise our substrate content assumptions
for select chips based on our latest supply chain checks, lifting our substrate demand
estimates across some segments, including server CPUs and networking.
Higher supply assumptions provide limited relief in CY27-28: We now include additional
capacity announcements that have been announced recently, including two additional

Kinsus factories, scheduled to come online in CY28 and CY29, beyond the CY27 expansion
already in our model. Several larger projects, however, contribute later. NYPCB’s new site
starts ramping in 2H29, while SEMCO’s Sejong expansion begins mass production in
September 2028 and its project in Vietnam is scheduled to begin operations by June
2030. These additions increase medium-term supply but do not prevent the modeled
shortfall from worsening through CY28.

Increasing substrate complexity adds to the pressure on usable output: Larger body
sizes, higher layer counts, and more demanding manufacturing processes constrain the
output available to meet demand, even as suppliers expand installed capacity. With the
supply gap widening in CY27-28, we expect tightness to become a more important pricing
driver than raw material cost pass-through. This supports our forecast for 25%+ price
increases in CY27 and further increases in CY28, alongside continued margin expansion.

Supply-side developments
Unimicron (3037.TW, OW, covered by Howard Kao)
On September 30, Unimicron announced that it has acquired a Hukou site from
Bridgestone Taiwan for ~NT$10.089bn (~US$318mn). Based on our checks, this is for
capacity expansion plans in 2030 or beyond.
For CY26, Unimicron has announced capex of NT$53.7B.

Over the next few years, Unimicron already has several new production facilities coming
online, including:
•  Guangfu II: ramp in 2027
• Yangmei II: ramp in 2028
• Yangmei III: ramp in 2029
• Yangmei IV: ramp in 2029+
• Bridgestone: ramp in 2030+

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
NYPCB (8046.TW, OW, covered by Howard Kao)
On August 6, NYPCB announced that its Board of Directors had approved a capex budget
of NT$46.8bn (~US$1,474mn) to invest in building a new ABF substrate production site.
Our checks indicate that this production site is located in the southern part of Taiwan, and
will start to ramp up production in 2H29. Given the size of this investment, we estimate
that it will approximately double its capacity exiting CY30, relative to its CY28 capacity.

Kinsus (3189.TW, Not Covered)
In our previous model, we had assumed only one factory (k6b) coming online in CY27,
increasing capacity ~30%. However, recent checks suggest that Kinsus intends to bring
two more factories online, one in CY28 (k6c) and one in CY29 (k6d). This implies that
capacity would increase another ~25% in CY28 and ~20% in CY29, bringing Kinsus' total
ABF substrate capacity to almost double exiting CY29, relative to its CY26 capacity.

Ibiden (4062.T, covered by Shoji Sato)
Back in February, Ibiden first announced that it will invest capex of ¥500bn in the
electronics field planned through F3/29, for ABF substrate expansion. Sato-san's latest
view is that Ibiden will need a new factory building to install the equipment and facilities
that will be purchased with the ¥500bn. As a result, Sato-san expects Ibiden during its 2Q
results briefing (October 30) to announce plans to make a large capital investment,

including for a factory building with a long lead time, and raise its medium-term earnings
forecast (report here).
SEMCO (009150.KS, covered by Shawn Kim)
On September 29, SEMCO announced investment of KRW4.27 trillion (~US$3,178mn) in
Sejong and KRW2.51 trillion (~US$1,488mn) in Vietnam, for AI server semiconductor
substrate capacity expansion.
• Sejong: Investment period from September 2026 to May 2028, with mass
production starting in September 2028. Largest single-product investment to date,

serving as key hub for mass production of high-value-added, highly demanding
products, proactively responding to the increasing demand for AI server substrates.
• Vietnam: The Vietnamese subsidiary aims to complete construction and begin
operations by June 2030. It will be operated as a strategic hub to respond to global
volume demand and enhance production efficiency through a stable supply
system.
With this investment, Samsung Electro-Mechanics plans to actively respond to the

growing demand for high-value-added semiconductor substrates in high-growth markets
such as AI servers, HPC, and automotive electronics. In particular, this investment is being
pursued based on medium- to long-term cooperation with global big tech and
semiconductor customers. The investment includes customer funding support and
medium- to long-term volume guarantees; thus, the company expects to strengthen the
competitiveness of its FCBGA business based on a stable demand foundation.

LG Innotek (011070.KS, covered by Ryan Kim)

### Page 5

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 5
According to news reports (link), LG Innotek signed a memorandum of understanding
(MOU) with Haiphong, Vietnam, for an investment to expand its semiconductor substrate
plant. Under the agreement, LG Innotek will push to build a semiconductor substrate
plant in Haiphong.
•  Construction will begin in July 2026 and is scheduled to be completed in May
2027.
• The site spans approximately 330,000 square meters (98,000 pyeong), equivalent

to 45 soccer fields.
• According to a news report, LG Innotek will invest approximately $1 billion, or
about 1.53 trillion won, for its Phase 1 expansion plan, which mainly includes RF-SiP
(radio frequency system-in-package) and FC-CSP (flip chip-chip scale package).
• ABF substrates will be part of the Phase 2 expansion plan, but no details or
investment size has been disclosed, yet.
According to a press release from LG Innotek on September 30, LG Innotek started mass

production of ABF substrates for PC chipsets targeting big tech clients last year. It
initiated high-volume mass production of FC-BGA for PC CPUs in the third quarter of this
year. Simultaneously, the company is rapidly boosting revenue by securing additional
major clients. Starting next year, it plans to sequentially enter the AI accelerator and AI
server markets.
• Revenue from LG Innotek's package solution business has maintained an upward
trajectory for three consecutive years. With the package solution business

demonstrating tangible results alongside its optical solution business, the
company has strengthened its capacity to generate stable earnings.
• LG Innotek plans to rapidly expand its market share in the emerging semiconductor
substrate market, aiming to build its package solution business, including
semiconductor substrates, into a business generating US$743 million in operating
profit by 2031.
• In the future, LG Innotek plans to develop its future businesses, including

semiconductor substrates and physical AI solutions, to US$5.94 billion by 2030.
The company aims to have such future new businesses account for 25% of total
revenue, thereby completing a well-balanced business portfolio.
The expanded plant will produce major semiconductor substrates including radio
frequency system-in-package (RF-SiP), flip chip chip scale package (FC-CSP), and flip chip
ball grid array (FC-BGA). "The Gumi site will serve as a 'mother factory' responsible for

developing new semiconductor substrate technologies and producing new models and
high-value-added products, while the expanded plant in Vietnam will be operated as a
production base for general-purpose semiconductor substrates," an LG Innotek official
explained.
AT&S (ATSV.VI, Not Covered)
On June 15, AT&S announced that it had signed key terms with AMD and Marvell to add
AI/HPC IC substrate capacity in Kulim, Malaysia. The company said the €1.5-2bn

investment (approximately US$1.68bn to US$2.24bn) is fully supported and financed by
long-term customer commitments, pending final execution.

Shinko (private)
On September 30, Shinko announced that it has entered into a purchase agreement with
Japan Display Inc. (JDI) to acquire its Mobara fab. In addition, it has entered into a sale
agreement with FICT Ltd. for the transfer of a portion of the fab to be acquired.
• Shinko will position the fab as a new core plant for advanced semiconductor
packages, and strengthen its production systems for flip-chip type packages
through the determination and implementation of capital investment plans for the

fab in conjunction with capacity expansion at its plants in the Nagano area.
Through these initiatives, Shinko aims to respond accurately to strong market
demand while pursuing sustainable business growth and enhancing corporate
value.
• The JDI Mobara fab has a total land area of 373,483 square meters, with Shinko
using the eastern area of the Fab (140,738 square meters, or ~38%) and FICT
planning to use the western area (232,745 square meters, or ~62%).

Toppan (7911.T, Not Covered)
According to news, Toppan broke ground in March 2024 on a new factory on Pesawat
Drive near Singapore's Jurong Lake District, built through its local unit Advanced Substrate
Technologies (AST), a JV with Broadcom. It makes FC-BGA substrates, which is Toppan's
term for them. It is Toppan's first FC-BGA plant outside Japan. The plant opening
ceremony took place on September 30, 2026.
• AST operates a facility of approximately 70,000 m² in the vicinity of Jurong Lake

District and employs more than 200 highly skilled engineers and technicians.
• Focus on high-demand, large-size, high-layer-count substrates
• While the investment size is not disclosed directly by the company, Nikkei sized
the investment at around ¥50 billion (~US$317mn).
Daeduck (353200.KS, Not Covered)
Daeduck announced on July 20, 2026, that it is investing ₩497 billion (~US$370M)
manufacturing facilities and related infrastructure for semiconductor products, running

through December 31, 2027.
ABF capex is stepping up, but announced investment still falls short of implied
demand. We estimate that ABF substrate makers have announced ~US$14.3bn of
investment year to date, materially above the segment’s aggregate annual capex in recent
years, although these commitments span multiple years. Our tally is not exhaustive. LG
Innotek has yet to disclose the size of its planned ABF capacity investment, while Shinko
has not quantified the additional spending required to expand capacity following its

announced acquisition of a JDI fab.
Our supply chain checks suggest that each US$1 of investment can support US$0.50–
0.60 of annual revenue at full ramp. On this basis, the US$14.3bn announced so far could
support US$7.2–8.6bn of incremental annual revenue. This compares with US$11.4bn of
incremental revenue implied by our industry forecast. We model the ABF substrate
market reaching US$20.9bn in 2030, up from US$9.5bn in 2026. Despite the step-up in

### Page 7

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 7
capex announcements, the investments we can currently quantify appear insufficient to
meet our demand forecast, pointing to a need for further capacity expansion over the
coming years.

Stronger ASIC demand from hyperscalers
We have updated our ASIC demand forecasts in our ABF model to align with the estimates

from our Semiconductors team. As shown in the exhibits below, our revised assumptions
point to a higher estimate for Amazon's Trainium processors in 2026, as well as marginally
lowered shipment estimates for Amazon's Trainium processors and Google's TPUs in 2027,
while the other  estimates  stay largely unchanged. These ASICs are characterized by large
die sizes and high layer-count package substrates, and thus we believe the slightly
lowered 2027 volume estimates do not derail our constructive outlook for the substrate

suppliers.

Morgan Stanley Research 13
The State of the Current Cycle
The ABF substrate market has gone through a few cycles over the past two
decades. We believe we are now still in the early innings of an up-cycle.
There was a prolonged down-cycle from 2012-18 as the PC TAM shrank amid the rise of
smartphones and tablets. During this tough period, companies were not able to generate
profits and had to compete on price to fill their idle capacity. As a result, during those

years, there was no or very limited capacity expansion, and capital expenditures by the
major ABF substrate suppliers were kept at a minimum.
Even as general server demand picked up, lifting utilization rates across the group going
into 2020, ABF substrate suppliers remained cautious on the longer-term demand outlook
and remained disciplined, hesitating to expand capacity. Hence, capex turned up just
slightly in 2019-20, increasing ~US$600mn vs. ~US$2.2bn on average per year in 2012-18.

This underinvestment led to sudden undersupply, as the shock from Covid-19 drove strong
work-from-home demand for PCs, as well as increased data usage and storage for general
computing driven by online entertainment. This ultimately led to a significant increase in
ABF substrate market value from 2020-22, reflected by the sudden surge in demand
along with meaningful pricing adjustments owing to the lack of supply.
During this 2020-22 period, much capacity expansion was set in motion as ABF substrate

suppliers and customers came to agree that necessary investments were needed for
future growth. Some subsidies were even given to these suppliers to facilitate this
capacity expansion. This is why capex jumped from ~US$2.9bn in 2020 to US$4.1bn in
2021 and US$5.5bn in 2022; even in 2023-24, capex remained elevated, as capacity
expansion plans continued.
Unfortunately, demand did not play out as expected. As the world came out of the
pandemic, demand for PCs and online entertainment fell sharply. This normalization of

demand, coupled with capacity expansion from practically every major ABF substrate
supplier, resulted in the ABF substrate market once again becoming oversupplied. This led
to a sharp decline in ABF substrate market value in 2023-24, as volumes normalized,
driving prices down to normalize against the new supply/demand dynamic.
Fortunately, cloud demand remained strong for general computing, and with AI demand
starting to pick up, the down-cycle this time around was much shorter (two years) vs. the

prior down-cycle (six years).
In 1H24, the ABF substrate market troughed and we saw an upward inflection since then,
but the pace of the recovery has been moderate and bifurcated. Midrange/low-end
markets such as PCs have shown moderate demand recovery, with high-end server and AI-
related end-demand recovering and growing at a faster pace. This is why we turned the
page on our bearish stance in 1H25, but did not turn more bullish until early 2026.

### Page 14

M
Idea
14
Periods Description
Period I (2010-2012): PC-centric
equilibrium
ABF substrate demand is primarily driven and dominated by CPU and GPU demand for desktops, notebooks,
discrete GPUs, with a meaningfully smaller exposure towards server CPUs.
Period II (2012-16): PC decline
and demand dilution
Global PC demand entered  a structural down-cycle as consumer behavior changed, with smartphones and tablets
absorbing more consumer time spent. This resulted in lower volumes and increased pricing competition as

suppliers fought to fill idle capacity.
Period III (2016-18): The quiet
inflection - servers and early
HPC
During these years, the PC market matured, while cloud computing started to take off. At the same time, server
CPU substrates quietly grew larger with more layers. We also started to see early accelerators being adopted in
high performance computing.
Period IV (2018-2022): WFH
demand driven structural
shortage
The Covid-19 pandemic drove massive work from home demand for consumer electronics and cloud computing.

This sudden surge in PC demand and increased time spent on gaming and online entertainment, bundled with a
continuous increase in substrate package size and layer count, resulted in a structural shortage, which also resulted
in longer lead times and raised prices. It was during this period (2021-22) that ABF suppliers globally started to
invest in new capacity.
Period V (2022-2024): Post-
pandemic correction and new
capacity arrival
As the world came out of the Covid-19 pandemic, WFH demand faded and  PC and cloud computing demand were

negatively affected. But at the same time, the new investments and capacity additions were just starting to slowly
come online, resulting in a drop in utilization rates and pricing.
Period VI (2024-2026):
Bifurcated recovery
PC demand normalized at the pre-Covid levels, with some replacement demand driven by Windows upgrade, while
the recovery and growth of AI and general server demand for cloud picks up at a meaningfully higher growth
trajectory.

Period VII (2026-30): AI diffusion Demand for AI proliferates from training to inference, driving ABF substrate market growth to re-accelerate over the
next five years after a two-year down-cycle following the Covid-19 pandemic. This drives increased demand for AI
computing, networking, and cloud computing, while demand for PCs remains soft.

Breaking down ABF substrate end demand
The end market mix for ABF substrates has shifted dramatically over the past decade. PCs

were once the dominant demand driver, accounting for approximately 70% of global ABF
substrate market value in 2015, supported primarily by PC CPUs and GPUs. Since then,
however, the industry's demand profile has undergone a structural transformation. We
estimate that PC-related applications represented only ~23% of total ABF substrate
market value in 2025, and this share is expected to decline further to below 10% by 2030.
In contrast, demand from servers, AI GPUs, AI ASICs, and networking applications has

expanded rapidly. We estimate that these segments collectively accounted for only
around 10% of the ABF substrate market in 2015, but their share increased to ~60% by
2025. Looking ahead, we expect they will represent more than 80% of total ABF
substrate market value by 2030.
In our view, this structural shift in end-market demand has been the primary driver of ABF
substrate industry growth over the past decade and will become even more pronounced

over the next five years. As AI infrastructure deployment accelerates, we expect continued
expansion in server, AI accelerator, and networking demand to drive meaningful
acceleration in ABF substrate market growth.

Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our 12-month price target increases ~21% to NT$1,775 (from NT$1,465), driven by our
increased earnings estimates for CY26-28: We believe the company's earnings have
returned to a growth track from 2026 onward and will show stronger  growth in 2027,
driven by the high-end ABF substrate market's reversion to undersupply and Unimicron's
dominant supply share in AI ASICs and also server CPUs, which puts it in a favorable

position to benefit from rising AI investments.
We believe the overall ABF substrate market bottomed in 2H24, and see growth
accelerating from 2H26 onward with the ramp of multiple next-generation, large body size
ABF substrates for new server GPUs, CPUs, ASICs, and networking chips. New
technologies such as embedded passives and Intel's EMIB-T should also increase the
manufacturing complexity of the package substrates, which adds to the under-capacity as

usable output decreases.
Similar to our analysis of other tech hardware companies within our coverage, we use a
residual income (RI) valuation model to value Unimicron – we think it derives the most
accurate value of the firm as it takes into account cost of equity. Our key assumptions
include:
• Cost of equity of 9.2% (unchanged)
° Risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield);
° Equity risk premium of 8.7%;
° Beta of 1.0;
• Medium-term growth rate of 14% (slightly lowered from 15%)

• Terminal growth rate of 3% (unchanged, similar to the rest of the companies under
our tech hardware coverage).
Our bull and bear case values increase by similar magnitudes, to NT$3,030 and NT
$595, respectively.

Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our price target increases ~29%, to NT$2,000, reflecting our raised earnings
estimates for CY26-28: We believe the company's earnings have returned to a growth
track from 2026 onward and will show stronger growth in 2027, driven by the high-end
ABF substrate market's reversion to undersupply, NYPCB's dominant supply share for
networking chips, and its proactive pricing strategy, which puts it in a favorable position to

benefit from rising AI investments.
We believe the overall ABF substrate market bottomed in 2H24, and growth should
accelerate from 2H26 onward with the ramp of multiple next-generation, large body size
ABF substrates for new server GPUs, CPUs, ASICs and networking chips. New
technologies, such as embedded passives and Intel's EMIB-T, should also increase
manufacturing complexity of the package substrates, adding to the overall undercapacity
as usable output decreases.

Similar to our analysis of other tech hardware companies within our coverage, we use a
residual income (RI) valuation model to value NYPCB – we think it derives the most
accurate value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. Key assumptions
(all unchanged):
• Cost of equity of 9.3%;
° Risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield);
° Equity risk premium of 8.7%;
° Beta of 1.0;
• Medium-term growth rate of 17%  –  fair given our analysis of the substrate market

over the next couple of years;
• Terminal growth rate of 3% (similar to the rest of the companies under our tech
hardware coverage).
Our bull and bear case values increase by similar magnitudes, to NT$3,575 and NT
$1,020, respectively.

▪ We expect the ABF substrate market to
revert to undersupply from 2027 onward,
with the undersupply gap reaching 26% by
2030.
▪ This is primarily driven by AI demand, with
next-gen AI/networking chips adopting
larger body size ABF substrates.
▪ In the near to medium term, the short
supply of T-glass will be a key watch point,
but we believe this will mainly affect low-

end ABF and BT substrates, as suppliers
prioritize supply to the AI chip vendors.
▪ Our price target implies 41x 2027e P/E or
24x 2028e P/E, above the 10-20x range at
which it traded in the previous up-cycle. We
deem this fair, given AI chip substrate spec
upgrades and suppliers' ability to raise
prices.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

大立光(3008) 
買進, TP 7,800 (潛在報酬 17.12%)  
買進

大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響 ， 加上新產
品良率及綠電採購成本壓力 ， 預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對
保守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司
發揮精密光學設計與製造優勢；另一方面， FAU 預計於 2H27
進入量產，隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來
源。 考量大立光具備高精度光學製造、 微米級對位技術及自動化
設備開發能力， 技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢， 正面看待公
司中長期發展。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入
為 706.92 億元，Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，
營業利益率 40.04%，Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，
Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。研究部考量儘管 2026 年消費
性電子需求疲弱，且 CPO 產品短期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度
優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，2027 年營運有望重回成長
軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目標價訂為 7,800 元，投
資建議為買進。  
⚫ 公司基本資料 
⚫ Largan 大立光電(3008.TT)公司簡介 
大立光電(3008.TT)成立於 1987 年，並於 2002 年台灣證券交易所
掛牌上市。大立光作為全球光學塑膠鏡頭領導廠商，主要業務內容
為研發與製造精密光學塑膠鏡頭，產品主要應用於智慧型手機，其
餘也包含平板、筆記型電腦、車用及其他消費性電子產品，在全球
光學鏡頭市占率約佔 30%。其總部位於台中市南屯區精密機械園
區，共有十座廠區及兩座興建中廠區。前十大股東持股合計
38.06%。客戶為 APPLE、華為、三星、小米、OPPO、VIVO 等手
機品牌廠，主要競爭者則為玉晶光 (3406.TT) 及舜宇光學
(2382.HK)。3Q26 出貨量佔比：20MP 以上約 10~20%、10MP 約

⚫ 產業概況 
記憶體成本飆升壓抑出貨，智慧型手機市場進入量縮價增 
根據研調機構數據 ，2026 年全球智慧型手機市場受記憶體供應短缺
及成本大幅上漲影響 ， 出貨量預估將Y-16.7%至 10.5 億支 ， 較前一
季預估的 Y-13.9%再度下修，並創下歷年最大年度衰退幅度，其中
2H26 出貨量更預估 Y-27.2%。 由於記憶體成本年增超過300%，品
牌廠難以自行吸收成本，陸續透過漲價及調整產品組合因應，帶動
2026 年智慧型手機平均售價 Y+27.6%至 581 美元，全球市場產值
反而 Y+6.3%至 6,130 億美元，呈現「量縮價增」格局。產品結構
方面，低階市場受到衝擊最為明顯，尤其100 美元以下機種因原先
毛利空間有限，品牌廠陸續縮減產品線，2Q26 該價位出貨量已年
減近 60%；相較之下，高階機種因消費者價格敏感度較低，且成熟
市場具分期付款等機制支撐，需求相對具韌性，帶動整體市場加速
朝高階化發展。 
展望未來，記憶體供需緊張預期至少延續至 2028 年，全球市場短

⚫ 未來展望 
拉貨動能逐步趨緩，良率改善有望緩解毛利率壓力 
展望 4Q26，預期 10 月拉貨量與 9 月持平，11 月起則隨新機拉貨
高峰結束而逐步下滑 ， 儘管客戶1H27 新產品已自 10 月開始拉貨，
仍難以完全彌補既有訂單下修影響，加上目前客戶需求能見度偏
短，第四季營收動能仍具不確定性。毛利率方面，儘管新機生產良
率逐步改善 ， 然而綠電採購成本持續增加 ， 相關成本最快須至2028
年才能反映於報價，預期短期毛利率改善幅度仍將受到限制。綜合
以上 ， 研究部預估大立光4Q26 營業收入為 155.21 億元 ，Y-9.86%、
Q-0.83%，毛利率 46.65%，Y-1.32ppts、Q+4.44ppts，營業利益
率 35.29%，Y-1.46ppts、Q+5.04ppts，稅後純益 54.91 億元，
Y-18.28%，EPS 為 41.98 元。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2026
年營業收入為 603.80 億元 ，Y-1.26%， 毛利率46.84%，Y-3.59ppts，
營 業 利 益 率 35.06% ，Y-3.47ppts ， 稅 後 純 益 214.67 億元，
Y+0.90%，EPS 為 164.12 元。

手機主鏡頭規格持續升級，高階光學產品支撐營運動能 
展望 2027 年，智慧型手機主鏡頭將有規格升級，帶動高階鏡頭設
計提升，目前相關產品已完成技術準備並持續送樣，有望隨新機導
入逐步貢獻營收。潛望式鏡頭方面，下一波較大幅度規格升級預計
落在 2028 年，目前已進入試產階段，2027 年則以既有產品出貨為
主。獲利方面，因綠電採購成本持續增加，短期毛利率改善仍將取
決於新產品良率及產品組合變化。

FA 預計 2H27 量產，精密對位與整合能力開啟光通訊新成長動能 
光通方面，FA 產品已通過客戶認證，FAU 則持續進行送樣及整合
驗證 ，FAU 預計 2027 年年中完成產線建置 ，2H27 正式進入量產。
初期以單排 FA 及 Grating Couplin 技術為主，並朝 FAU 與 Carrier
整合出貨發展 ， 提高單一產品附加價值 ， 多排FA 及 Edge Coupling

自製自動化設備及檢測能力 ， 可望在高技術門檻的FAU 市場建立競
爭優勢。預期 2027 年光通營收貢獻仍處於起步階段，隨後續產能
與良率提升，有望成為中長期成長動能。綜合以上，研究部預估大

億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。

⚫ 結論與建議 
大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響，加上新產品
良率及綠電採購成本壓力，預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對保
守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司發揮
精密光學設計與製造優勢 ； 另一方面 ，FAU 預計於 2H27 進入量產 ，
隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來源。考量大立
光具備高精度光學製造、微米級對位技術及自動化設備開發能力，
技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢，正面看待公司中長期發展。綜
合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入為 706.92 億元，
Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，營業利益率 40.04%，
Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08
元。研究部考量儘管2026 年消費性電子需求疲弱 ，且CPO 產品短
期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，

標價訂為 7,800 元，投資建議為買進。

### Page 1

EQUITY: FABLESS
Global Unichip Corp   3443.TW 3443 TT
Global Markets Research
19 October 2026
Riding the next megatrend of SoIC; maintain Buy
Several key CSP projects in the pipeline to support growth until 2028-29F
Benefiting from SoIC — the next megatrend in advanced packaging, in our view
GUC shares have rallied ~100% since late July (vs the TAIEX up 11%), driven by a strong 
wave of consensus earnings upward revisions (Fig. 11). This is largely in line with our 
observations highlighted in our 2 Aug report that “consensus expectations were way too low.” 
We believe the Street is starting to acknowledge that there is a strong pipeline of CSP projects 
over the next 2-3 years, including Google CPU (GOOGL US, Not rated; revenue sharply 
higher in 2026 with momentum potentially sustaining into 2027F and 2028F as Axion 3 
doubles silicon content from Axion 2), as well as the other top three US hyperscalers’ ASIC 
and CPU projects along with Tesla’s (TSLA US, Not rated) AI5/AI6.5. GUC not only has 
secured projects from top US hyperscalers, but at the same time acquired additional 
meaningful customers with volume globally, e.g. China auto ASIC and US AI ASIC using a 
SoW approach. However, among all, the one that has caught our attention the most is the 
Google TPU v.10 compute die opportunity (though we believe the turnkey deal is yet to be 
finalized) — not only because the addressable size could be significant, but this business 
opportunity might originate from GUC’s G-link 3D die-on-die IP. (Fig. 1 Fig. 4) We raise our 
2026-28F EPS for GUC by 15%-37% to incorporate business upside from non-Axion projects, 
and raise TP to TWD9,600 (from TWD5,800), based on an unchanged 40x target P/E, while 
rolling forward valuation to the 2027-28F average EPS. We are yet to incorporate overseas 
convertible bonds (ECB) in our model/EPS estimates.
The rising importance of SoIC in advanced packaging should benefit GUC, given its 
leading investments in Glink-3D die-on-die IP among design service peers
SoIC is not new, but it was small before 2026 given limited adoption by AMD MI300 chip (AMD 
US, Not rated) and Apple M-series chip (AAPL US, Not rated). In 2026, nVidia’s (NVDA US, Not 
rated) demand for CPO using TSMC’s (2330 TT, Buy) COUPE (EIC on PIC stacking) has further 
lifted SoIC demand. In our June anchor report, we noted that nVidia’s Feynman could become 
the first GPU-on-GPU stacking SoIC (along with SiC carrier) AI chip. Since then, several US CSP 
ASIC projects have also planned to enable SoIC die stacking in their chip roadmap, according to 
our industry checks. GUC’s Glink-3D IP (see Fig. 1- Fig. 4) appears well positioned to ride this 
trend as it has developed this IP along with TSMC’s roadmap and currently a number of CSPs 
are using its IP to build their test chip – which in the long run could prove to be a good stepping 
stone for GUC to win more services, in our view. Besides, GUC’s SoIC IP would also be 
deployed with TSMC’s scale-up CPO customers (e.g. AyarLabs, Fig. 5).

### Page 3

Company profile
Established in 1998, GUC is one of the leading design service companies in Taiwan with self-developed IPs. TSMC is GUC's single-largest
shareholder with a 35% share.
Valuation Methodology
Our TP of TWD9,600 is based on 40x 2027-28F average EPS (40x P/E is its historical mid level). The benchmark index for the stock is TAIEX.
Risks that may impede the achievement of the target price
Downside risks include 1) fierce ASIC competition, 2) slowdown of key projects and lack of capacity, and 3) more insourcing trend from customers

ESG
GUC has six major ESG strategies to achieve the goals of sustainable development, including corporate governance, innovative R&D, partnership
prosperity, environmental sustainability, friendly workplace, and social participation.

Valuation methodology and risks
Our TP of TWD9,600 is based on 40x 2027-28F average EPS (40x P/E is its historical 
mid level). The benchmark index for the stock is the TAIEX.

lack of capacity, and (3) increased in-sourcing from customers.

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

## Document 303: Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

7 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES

In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.

## Document 308: France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

2027 Budget: Fiscal consolidation must now resume

The 2027 budget, adopted by the French Council of Ministers today, calls for a reduction in France’s fiscal 
deficit to 5% of GDP in 2027 (down from 5.4% in 2026). This is a first step toward s the multi-year fiscal 
consolidation needed to stabilise the public debt-to-GDP ratio. This ratio is expected to rise further in 2027 
and until the deficit is reduced to 3% of GDP. Furthermore, given the increase in some expenditures (+0.6 pp 
of GDP for debt service and defence spending), the projected fiscal effort amounts to 1 pp. Nearly one-third 
of this effort would come from higher compulsory levies, and two -thirds from curbing government and 
social spending.

2027 Draft finance law: A step in the right direction. The government is forecasting an improvement in the fiscal 
deficit from 5.4% of GDP in 2026 to 5% in 2027. This projection is based on a credible growth assumption of 1% 
(see our analysis of French growth drivers). To achieve this target, compulsory levies (CL) are expected to rise to 
44.2% of GDP, an increase of 0.3 pp (the same increase as in 2026, compared with +0.9 pp in 2025). The public 
spending-to-GDP ratio would decline by 0.2 pp to 56.9%. The effort required to reduce the deficit to 5% is greater 
than it appears. Indeed, some expenditures will increase (interest payments by 0.4 pp and defence spending by 
0.2 pp). Consequently, the fiscal effort for next year amounts not to 0.4 pp, but to 1 pp of GDP. One-third of this will 
be achieved through increased CL, and two-thirds through spending cuts. Social spending will contribute, notably 
through the cancellation of EUR 3 billion in social contribution reductions for low-wage earners, as well as efforts 
totaling EUR 6 billion each in the areas of pensions and healthcare. Social spending shows the largest deviation 
from pre-COVID levels, with an increase representing 1.2 pp of GDP, nearly half of the gap between the 2026 
budget balance and a 3% deficit. Government spending, excluding interest payments and defence, is expected to 
be frozen in nominal terms.

A compromise seems likely despite a tight schedule. The French National Assembly will start reviewing the 
budget on Tuesday 13 October. The government will need to ensure that a majority vote against the draft budget 
does not form. The current circumstances, including increasing sovereign bond yields and the upcoming 
presidential election, call for a consensus based on fiscal consolidation of the size proposed by the government. If 
Parliament does not pass a budget by the end of the year, the government will have to choose between 
implementing the government’s budget proposal by executive order ( only possible if there was no parliamentary 
vote) or through a special law (as in 2025 and 2026). A special law would enable tax collection , while the 
government and social security agencies could continue to issue debt. In this case, the 2026 budget would apply 
in 2027 (without indexation) until Parliament passes a budget in the first few weeks of the year. This must occur 
before the end of February (when Parliament’s session ends, earlier than usual due to the presidential election).

The one-year postponement of the 5% budget deficit target has a measurable impact. The gap between the 
budget adopted for 2026 (deficit of 5% of GDP) and the actual budget outcome (deficit of 5.4%) is due to 
unforeseeable circumstances (the conflict in Iran  and extreme drought). However, postponing the deficit target  
previously set for 2026 to 2027 comes at a cost: 1) the public debt-to-GDP ratio is expected to reach 121% in 2027 
according to our forecasts (1 pp above our scenario with a 5% target in 2026 and a deficit reduced to 4.5% in 2027), 
and 2) the (upcoming) postponement to 2032 of the 3% of GDP deficit target (a commitment that most presidential 
candidates plan to make) rather than in 2029 (the government had committed to this with the European Commission 
in 2024). In 2032, the public debt-to-GDP ratio would stabilise at 124% of GDP (instead of 121.5% if the deficit had 
been reduced to 5% of GDP as early as 2026 and to 3% of GDP by the end of the decade).

Another delay in fiscal consolidation would further erode France’s credibility. This new delay could stem 
either from a decision by the government formed in May 2027 to immediately implement part of its agenda, or from 
the enactment of a special law that would remain in effect until (just after) the likely legislative elections in June  
2027  or  for  the  entire  year.  If  the  government  emerging  from  the  elections  were  to  resume  fiscal  
consolidation only in 2028 (with a deficit of 5% of GDP that it projects to reduce to 3% by 2032), public debt would 
reach 126% of GDP by that date (a one-year delay in fiscal consolidation would result in an additional 2 pp of 
debt). This further delay could potentially undermine the credibility of the next government, given that budget 
targets were  not met in three out of four years between 2023 and 2026, a situation that would be costly amid a 
sharp rise in long-term interest rates. Furthermore, a higher deficit would increase the amount of debt to be issued 
within an already  tight schedule. The Agence France Trésor (AFT) has announced debt issuances totaling EUR 
340 billion in 2027 (an  increase  of  EUR  20  billion  compared  to  2026).  This  increase  is  due  to  the  maturing  
COVID  related  debt (beginning in 2027 and continuing largely through 2030), while the amount of debt to be 
issued to finance the deficit will remain stable compared to 2026. A decrease from this last driver would be needed 
in subsequent years so that  the AFT’s debt issuance can stabilise.

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of the Advanced Economies Team, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com
