## Document 320: How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

SPECIAL EDITION | HOW BAS IS THE RISE 
IN BOND YIELDS?

TRANSCRIPTION

0:00:10 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Everyone, welcome. As we head into autumn 2026, interest rates are reaching levels never seen for many, many years. 
Why is this happening? Is it sustainable? And what are the implications for the economies of different countries? All these 
questions will be discussed over the next few minutes with the economists of BNP Paribas. Let us begin with Isabelle 
Mateos y Lago, chief economist of the group. Hello, Isabelle.  
0:00:38 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everyone. 
0:00:39 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you for being with us today. Just to begin, a simple explanation. What rates are we talking about and which countries 
are affected? 
0:00:48 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, it is very important to clarify this. So, we are talking about long-term interest rates, rates at 10 years or longer 
maturities, 30 years, etc. The rates at which sovereign states borrow on the market. And this is primarily something that is 
happening in advanced countries, so United States, United King dom, Japan, and then the Eurozone countries. And indeed, 
as you said, we are seeing since the beginning of the summer, interest rates reach levels we had not seen for about 20 
years. But it is important to keep in mind that up until that point, up until the mid -2000s, the level of interest rates that 
we're seeing now were really quite common, quite normal, so one shouldn't overdramatize.  
0:01:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why do we find ourselves in this situation and how did we get to this point?  
0:01:41 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, so the direct cause is that investors are demanding higher interest rates in order to  lend their capital to governments. 
But why is this? Well, there's essentially three reasons for this, and not all of them are bad, and they're not mutually 
exclusive either. The top reason to my mind is that there's just greater competition for capital be tween sovereign states 
and the corporate sector, who are all facing simultaneously additional investment needs, in defense, in artificial 
intelligence, in energy transition, in resilience. And so,  you just have greater competition for capital and that pushes up the 
price of capital, which is the interest rate. 
Reason number two is because markets have revised upwards their expectations of how high central banks are going to be 
taking their interest rates. 
And this mechanically translates into higher rates at the long end. And then there's a third reason, which doesn't apply 
necessarily in every case, but which is essentially higher sovereign risk, a kind of a risk premium, in cases where there are  
concerns arising about the sustainability of these public debts.

### Page 2

2

0:02:59 - Emmanuel Laborde 
What are the implications for global economies? 
0:03:03 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
So that's exactly what we'll be discussing in the next segments. But basically, the two key issues are, one, an adverse 
impact on public finances. It's higher interest rates making it harder to balance the books in terms of public accounts. And 
then secondly, these higher rates tend to slow down economic growth. Of course, by how much and how serious it will be, 
depends on how high interest rates go and how long they stay there. But that's for the next segments.  
0:03:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Yes. Thank you very much, Isabelle. Having said the scene now, let's move on to the first segment with the following 
question. What makes debt sustainable? And let's see what Hélène Baudchon has to say.  
Hello Hélène and welcome into this special edition. 
0:03:58 - Hélène Baudchon 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everybody. 
0:04:01 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When economists talk about public debt, they often refer to the sustainability of debt. As an economist, can you explain to 
us what that means and what is the debt trajectory deemed unsustainable?  
0:04:15 - Hélène Baudchon 
To begin with, I will say that a temporary increase in debt can be seen as a normal evolution, not particularly concerning, 
for instance during an economic crisis or more recently during a health crisis. In such cases, it acts as a buffer. A problem  
arises when the debt level never comes back down. An unsustainable trajectory can be therefore defined, characterized by 
a continuous regular rise in the debt level, both in absolute terms and as a proportion of GDP, driven by high and recurring 
fiscal deficits. And sometimes interest payments can exacerbate the problem.  
0:05:00 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In very simple terms, you are talking about the snowball effect.  
0:05:03 - Hélène Baudchon 
Exactly explaining why consolidation measures are needed in order to prevent such an adverse situation from occurring 
and in order to prevent, in fact, a sudden loss of confidence from creditors.  
0:05:16 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Regarding governments, what should serve as a warning sign for them? And at what point could the situation become, let's 
say, problematic? 
0:05:27 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, simply speaking, when interest rates rise, not so long ago, up until 2022, Eurozone countries were borrowing at very 
low rates and in some instance, even negative rates. And although debt levels were already at high level at that time, the 
low rates helped manage debt servicing. Not much fiscal effort was needed to keep the public debt ratios in check.  
0:05:55 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Of course, we all remember this because it was a very unusual situation, but now things are very different.

### Page 3

3

0:06:01 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed, the situation today is clearly more challenging simply because bond yields are significantly higher and they 
are rising at the time of our discussion. This is for the part the result of the ECB's monetary tightening in 2022 -2023 aimed 
at addressing the inflation shock then. Then long-term interest rates have risen as a consequence and have aligned more 
closely with GDP growth rate. And in technical terms, this is what we refer to as a narrowing of the "r" - "g" spread. 
0:06:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why is this spread so important? Why do you, the economists, monitor it this way?  
0:06:48 - Hélène Baudchon 
Essentially, you have the interest rate "r", which determines the debt burden, while the growth rate "g" determines the 
trajectory of fiscal revenues. And if "r" is lower than "g", and this has been the case for many years, as you can see on the  
chart on the screen, it means that interest payments are increasing at a slower pace than the revenue component of the 
equation. 
0:07:14 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this provides some leeway in other budgetary areas, such as education, of course, defense, maybe pensions.  
0:07:22 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed. And this is what we refer to as the primary budget. However, if and when interest rates do go up while the 
economy isn't growing that much faster, then fiscal leeway diminishes.  
0:07:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Crystal clear. Now, just could you provide a few last words to wrap up the sustainability segment?  
0:07:43 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, to conclude, we are entering a period which demands greater fiscal discipline or, as some might say, less indiscipline 
in order to manage debt trajectories effectively, in particular in the countries which have high debt levels. And for instanc e, 
the Italian case is an interesting one because it is well known for its high debt level ratio. But in fact, it also has an 
advantageous position because it has no primary deficit. In fact, it even benefits from a large primary surplus.  
0:08:22 - Emmanuel Laborde 
As for the countries... For the other countries... I stop you because this will be a discussion with Guillaume Derrien during  
the next segment. Thank you very much, Hélène. 
0:08:30 - Hélène Baudchon 
Thank you, Emmanuel. 
0:08:36 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Guillaume. Welcome. 
0:08:37 - Guillaume Derrien 
Thank you. 
0:08:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Emmanuel, and hello to everyone. The flexibility of fiscal policy depends heavily on interest rates, which have 
increased considerably over the past few months. The question is, where do we stand today?

### Page 4

4

0:08:52 - Guillaume Derrien 
So, long-term rates have increased in all advanced economies, reaching levels not seen since 2008 in Europe and in the 
United States, and even since 1996 in Japan. Fortunately, the effective rate that states pay on their debt is lower than thos e 
headline figures suggest, simply because the average maturity of debt is relatively long. Precisely how long are we talking 
about? So, it's about 10 years in Japan, 13 years in the United Kingdom, and 8.5 years in France. That means that most of 
the debt was raised before rates started climbing. 
For example, in France, the effective rate just recently rose above the 2% mark, which is significantly lower than the 10 -
year bond yield, which exceeded 4% for new issuances.  
That said, in the United States, which has an average maturity of roughly six years, is experiencing a more rapid increase in  
its effective rate. 
0:09:54 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this rate increase is happening in the context of high debt -to-GDP ratios and with modest growth, correct? Yes, that's 
correct. 
0:10:05 - Guillaume Derrien 
Debt levels have reached unprecedented heights since the end of World War II. And at the same time, growth is lower, 
hovering around 2% on average in the United States, 1% in Europe and 0.5% in Japan. What does that mean for debt 
servicing costs? So, debt servicing cost is already relatively high, but it is expected to grow further as debt issue before the 
2022 rate hikes mature and are replaced with new instruments bearing higher interest rates. By 2030, interest costs could 
rise to levels last seen in 2000, specifically in France, around 4% of GDP. 
Are all the countries equally affected by these trends? Actually, no. Some countries are managing to reduce their debt 
level. 
Spain, Italy, even Japan have sufficiently improved their primary balance to achieve this, at least for the time being. And o f 
course, we must speak about Germany. Yes, Germany is implementing investment programs that result in a higher public 
deficit and debt-to-GDP ratio. But this ratio started from a relatively low base at around above 60% of GDP. That said, in 
this current context, German bonds have risen. And because German bonds are used as a benchmark for Europe, it  raises 
rates across all Eurozone countries. Is France the only country seeing its public debt go up?  
No, it's not, Emmanuel. This is happening in the United Kingdom and in the United States. These economies, like France, 
are running public deficits that are ever close or exceed 5% of GDP, which is too large for debt ratio to stabilize on its ow n. 
France and the United Kingdom are trying to gradually reduce their public deficit so as not to compromise growth. The 
United States is not going on that path yet, but market pressure could eventually force them to do so.  
0:12:08 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much for your input, Guillaume. Thank you, Emmanuel. Let's go to the next segment. It's about the impact 
of rising bond yields on financing and investments in the Eurozone.  
0:12:26 - Thomas Humblot 
Hello, Thomas. Welcome. Hello. Thank you for having me today.  
0:12:30 - Emmanuel Laborde 
We've just seen what's behind the rise in Eurozone bond yields. This rise is partly attributed to the sovereign spread. What 
impact will this have on the economy? 
0:12:42 - Thomas Humblot 
Actually, this widening of some government bond spreads is obviously not good news, but it tends paradoxically to have 
less impact than an increase in long-term interest rates that does not include the sovereign risk premium. for example, in 
France, for a very long time, it has been considered that the 10 -year OAT was the relevant benchmark for fixed loans. And 
at that time, the spread on French government bonds was relatively stable. But since 2024, that spread increased

### Page 5

5

significantly and that renders the sovereign yield a less relevant benchmark.  
And now the most relevant benchmark is the swap rate because it determines the cost of bank funding that backs new 
loans. 
0:13:56 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Since the end of 2024, we have seen a rise in long-term 10-year swap rates. What impact does this have on bank lending 
rates? 
0:14:08 - Thomas Humblot 
Fixed loans have obviously become more expensive, but to a lesser extent than if they were priced solely based on long -
term sovereign rates. And what about existing fixed-rate loans? 
There is no impact. An increase in rates for the entire maturity of a loan does not have any effect if it is a fixed -rate loan. 
Borrowers with outstanding loans are therefore protected. Obviously, new fixed loans are granted at higher rates. And this 
sharply contrasts with a floating-rates loans. 
Because with floating-rates loans, borrowers experience regular rate adjustments throughout the term of the loan. And in 
terms of bank financing? Strictly in terms of bank financing, an increase in short -term rates has an immediate and 
widespread restrictive impact on borrowers, while for long-term interest rates, the impact is more gradual, and it comes 
gradually as the outstanding amount of fixed-rate loans are progressively renewed. And the thing is that since the 
beginning of the war in Iran, we have seen a combination of the two.  
And in practical terms, the rate of new bank loans to non-financial corporations has increased about 25 basis points in the 
eurozone since the beginning of the year. 
0:16:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Just a few words about companies that borrow directly from the market.  
0:16:16 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, in that case, the impact is even more straightforward. The rate at which a company borrows from the market is the 
risk-free rate plus a premium. 
0:16:28 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Have risk premiums, known as corporate credit spreads, also increased?  
0:16:34 - Thomas Humblot 
Yes, they have increased temporarily at the beginning of the year but have returned to normal since. For the time being, the 
widening remains moderate for the highest-rated investment-grade issuers. 
However, some significant bond issuance in the euro debt market due to some major US technology companies has 
increased spreads for longer maturities. 
0:17:11 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In particular terms, how does this affect the investment decisions of the companies?  
0:17:19 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, investments that were previously worthwhile due to returns sufficient to cover the cost of capital may no longer do so 
and in turn that might lead to a decrease in demand for financing.  
0:17:40 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much Thomas. Now let us move on to the emerging economies. And it is with Cynthia Kalasopatan -Antoine.

### Page 6

6

Hello Cynthia, and welcome. 
0:17:54 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Hi, Emmanuel. Hello, everyone. 
0:17:57 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Long-term bond yields rose significantly in advanced economies over the summer. What can we now say about emerging 
markets? 
0:18:06 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Emerging bond markets held up well, which is quite unusual. The rise in long -term sovereign yields in emerging countries 
has been less pronounced than in advanced economies, including the United States and Germany. Now, there are two 
main exceptions, Turkey and Poland. Meanwhile, we did not witness any significant correction in emerging financial 
markets over the summer, and the main currencies appreciated slightly against the dollar or the euro.  
0:18:37 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Does this mean that public finances are more robust in emerging countries?  
0:18:43 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, it depends. You see, in cases like the Czech Republic and South Korea, public debt is moderate and macroeconomic 
fundamentals are solid. However, these countries are now less frequently considered as emerging and tend to be viewed 
as newly advanced economies. By contrast, Brazil and South Africa have weaker public finances and investors have 
already incorporated a higher sovereign risk into their assessments. And this is reflected in the bond yields. And this 
assessment of sovereign risk did not really change over the summer. 
0:19:26 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When comparing the main fiscal metrics, what are the trends? What do you see as an economist?  
0:19:33 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
On average, the fiscal metrics in emerging markets are not substantially worse than those in advanced economies. The 
average budget deficit levels are similar, ranging from 4% to 5% of GDP in 2025 -2026, according to the IMF. And as in 
advanced economies, the public debt is higher than its pre-Covid level. However, the average public debt to GDP ratio in 
emerging countries excluding China is close to 60%, whereas in advanced economies, it is above 100%.  
0:20:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Do you arrive at the same conclusion when comparing debt sustainability criteria?  
0:20:17 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, there are significant regional differences. In emerging Asia, the factors that determine debt sustainability are more 
often favorable than those in advanced economies. In Central Europe, the gap between the interest rate paid on debt and 
the GDP growth rate is also favorable. However, the primary deficits in some countries are high.  
0:20:45 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In a few words, a brief overview of the situation in Latin America.  
0:20:49 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, Brazil and Mexico are in a less favorable position. These countries would indeed need to generate a significant 
primary budget surplus each year in order to stabilize their debt in the medium term.

### Page 7

7

0:21:07 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Economists use the term original sin, which refers to the situation when governments resort to borrowing in foreign 
currency to address the shortfall in domestic financing. Does this phenomenon continue to distinguish emerging 
countries? 
0:21:24 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Yes, this is still the case. Emerging countries still have a significant share of their debt in foreign currency. This repres ents 
about 35 to 40% of their total debt on average. And in fact, now, this figure has decreased over the past decade, and this 
contributes to their resilience. However, governments in emerging markets have a less favorable position as regards their 
debt profile and their financing costs are also on average higher.  
0:22:02 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And finally, just a few words about a larger subject, China.  
0:22:07 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
China's public debt is significant and growing rapidly. That being said, it is denominated in domestic currency and issued in  
domestic markets. This debt is, however, sustainable in the medium term due to a very low effective interest rate and a 
high growth rate. 
0:22:29 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Cynthia, for being with us today.  
0:22:32 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Thank you. 
0:22:33 - Emmanuel Laborde 
It is now time to conclude, and we welcome back Group Chief Economist Isabelle Mateos y Lago.  
It is time for our conclusion with Isabelle. Once again, just a final question, please. Could or should, I do not know which 
word is the good one, central banks intervene to curb this rise in interest rates?  
0:22:58 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Absolutely not. The main contribution that central banks can bring to dealing with this situation is to restore price stabili ty, 
as is their mandate, as promptly as possible. And in the current circumstances, this means keeping relatively high policy 
rates, in some cases, rising them further. And the responsibility for keeping public finances on a sustainable footing rests 
squarely with governments and any attempt by these governments to instead take the easy way out by pressuring central 
banks to lower interest rates prematurely would be enormously counterproductive.  
0:23:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Isabelle. Thank you all for joining us today. Just a few words to remind you that additional economic 
research content can be found by scanning the QR code shown on your screen. Do not hesitate to use it with your 
smartphone. It is very easy. It was a pleasure to be with you today. Have a nice day and hope to see you so

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026

高 。 繼上週歐洲 央 行 將 存 款 利 率 調 升 至
2.50%後，日本銀行 也加速升 息，基準利率

49票對50票未獲通過 ，無法進入最終表決程
序，虛擬資產聞訊下 挫。沙烏地阿拉伯關閉
東西輸油管線，佔全球原油供應 量約4-5%，

率利空出盡下滑，激勵金、銅價反彈。

► 聯準會9月升息1碼至3.75%-4.00%，最新點
陣圖顯示2026年仍有再升息一碼空間 ，2027
年傾向維持利率不變 ，2028年後才逐步回到
降息趨勢 。主席華許重申抑制通膨目標 ，升
息立場官員數明顯較前次會議增加 ，凸顯聯
準會鷹派政策態度 ，惟利率路徑較市場原先
預期的連續緊縮和緩，市場偏利多解讀。
► 聯準會 本次亦上修 2026-2027年GDP成長預
估，下修失業率預測 ，反映美國 經濟與勞動
市場尚未明顯轉弱；另一方面 ，能源價格上
升與成本轉嫁使整體及核心通膨預估再度上

修；景氣具韌性 、通膨回落速度偏慢 ，使聯
準會仍有條件維持較緊縮的政策立場 。在高
利率環 境可 能延 續 下，股 票評價 仍面 臨壓
力，配置上可相對偏向獲利與現金流穩定 、
防禦性較高的產業；債券則以中短天期 、高
信評企業債為主要方向 ，把握目前較高的收
益率。長天期公債仍受通膨 、財政紀律及期
限溢酬上升影響 ，宜待通膨與景氣出現更明
確降溫訊號後，再提高配置。

► 日本8月CPI年增1.9%，油價上漲帶來的外溢
影響，逐漸反應在消費成本上。7月勞工平均
薪資較去年同期成長 4.7%，創1997年1月以
來最大增幅；薪資增速追上通膨 ，帶動實質
薪資增長 。此外 ，第二季 GDP季增年率達
1.4%優於預期，8月製造業PMI因AI及半導體
需求暢旺上升，7月出口年增率高達23.2%，
創2022年10月來最快增速 。整體通膨升溫加
上景氣復甦，為日銀升息提供底氣。
► 日本央行會後 聲明稿表示：金融環境實質利
率偏低 ，將視情況調整貨幣政策 ，並強調升
息後金融環境仍維持寬鬆 ，未如市場預期的
鷹派，使日股持續上漲 、日圓回貶 。考量日
銀呵護 寬鬆金融環境 ，侷限日圓續強力 道，
但在美 國施 壓與 日 銀未來 仍可能 升息 背景
下，日圓難回到過去極弱 狀態。相較過去 ，
現在升值的日圓對日本出口 、科技股壓力可
控，日本10年公債殖利率來到3%，利率上升
有利 金融 股 ，投 資 短期或 以金融 、內 需為

主，科技、半導體股逢回可分批布局。
經濟與通膨升溫，日銀加快緊縮步調

投資焦點低波價值防禦
► 美、歐、日主要央行連袂升息 ，加上美伊衝
突惡化 ，國際油價高漲 ，通膨疑慮與成熟國
家債務壓力 ，使全球多數國家公債殖利率衝
高，市場擔憂高利率與高油價可能拖累經濟
成長，全球股債匯市承壓 、波動上升 ，並以
此前領漲多頭行情的晶片 、半導體和科技股
最明顯 。觀察今年以來 ，不止股市波動度上
升，債市的波動更不亞於 股票市場 ，統計過
去，當VIX恐慌指數與MOVE債券波動度走高
時，低波動或防禦股的表現 ，往往比成長股
更好，顯示當市場震盪加劇時 ，資金傾向轉
往穩健資產停泊。
► 最近一次聯準會升息 ，為2022 年因疫情爆
發、供應鏈中斷 ，導致通膨大幅上揚 ，迫使
央行採取緊縮貨幣政策 ，和目前通膨上升促
央行升息背景 類似。前次聯準會啟動升息後
數月，成長股 、費城半導體股表現受抑 ，防
禦股、價值股和低波動族群 ，在剛開始升息
的環境下 ，表現相對抗跌 。考量目前聯準 會
籠罩在升息陰霾下，可增加相關類股配置。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 最近一次聯準會升息後，防禦低波高息和價值股突出美國股債波動度上升時，低波動、防禦股表現較佳

金融市場波動上升，增配低波、防禦價值股更穩健

### Page 8

► 觀察最近一季 ，資訊科技 、通訊服務與非核
心消費等High Beta類股，表現明顯落後於大
盤；反觀價值股 、防禦股和低波動等因子 ，
甚至核 心消 費與 高 股利等 傳統類 防禦 性產
業，報酬相對較 穩健，受資金青睞 。顯示在
經歷過 上半 年科 技 、半導 體股的 大漲 行情
後，市場預期高 利率與地緣政治 風險可能維
持更久時間 ，重新聚焦在獲利持久、現金易
落袋為安的防禦型資產，更能持盈保泰。
► 從預估本 益比看，目前金融僅 16倍、防禦股
17.5倍、價值股 (以IWD ETF代表)17.9倍、
低波動 (以USMV ETF代表)19.6倍，皆低於
標普500指數 現在 的21.3倍，近月資金流向
物美價廉的低波動價值 、防禦族群，評價越
低、漲幅越高 。展望後市，AI長線基本面仍
持續看好 ，股市回檔或為評價 修正而 非盈餘
下滑，市場波動度提升之際 ，建議不過度 集
中於單一 產業或 個股，多元配置適度增加 低
波動 、 防禦 和價 值股，分 批布局 以控管 風

險，料將更能在震盪加劇時安穩求利。

### Page 9

股市
 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，利率創高添股市波動風險 ，加上成熟國家債務攀升 ，科技巨擘亦舉債
投入資本支出 ，未來獲利與變現 能力持續被市場檢視 ，金融市場震盪不減 。美伊衝突升溫，近期可
透過防禦、低波動或價值 股來降低風險；科技 及AI半導體等發展趨勢不變 ，獲利基本面佳 ，長線發
展可期，宜採逢回分批布局方式，分散國家與產業配置。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率彈升有利銀行股 ，歐、日銀行股基本面佳 ，歐洲國防軍工
仍具投資吸引力。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融等產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，應避免超長天期債券投資 ，預期年底前美國 10年期公債利率將於 4.5%-5.0%
震盪，可逢利率走升時，以中短天期債券進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款
能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。

債種：中天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金融、
科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息後，年底前或明年初仍可能再度緊縮，日圓震盪加大偏升。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪加大偏升
商品
 沙烏地阿拉伯關閉輸油管線 ，加上荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張 ，能源供需失衡 ，油價年底前維持 偏高水位。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力 與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。

### Page 16

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

### Page 1

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Stopping for gas
• We estimate that New Zealand consumer
prices rose by 0.7% in the September quarter,
with a moderation in fuel costs balanced
against increases in council rates, building
costs and food prices.
• The annual inflation rate is expected to drop
back to 3.7% (down from 4.1% in the year
to  June).
• Measures of core inflation are expected to
remain firm, with some closely watched
measures expected to rise to the top of the

RBNZ’s target band.
• Our forecast is a little below the RBNZ’s last
published forecast for 3.9% annual inflation.

We expect the September quarter inflation report (out
on 22 October) will show that New Zealand consumer
prices rose 0.7% over the past three months. That would
see the annual inflation rate slipping back to 3.7%, down
from 4.1% in the year to June. However, as we discuss

in our recent forecast update, that’s likely to be just a
temporary reprieve with the recent rise in oil prices set to
push inflation up again over the coming months.
16 Oct 2026 | Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
CPI preview, September quarter 2026 –
Thursday 22 October, 10:45am.

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Key drivers of quarterly inflation.
Underpinning our forecast for 0.7% inflation in the
September quarter are some big price movements in
both directions.
The largest move in prices this quarter has actually been
a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by any
stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for
both petrol and diesel over the September quarter were
lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed

higher in the wake of the Middle East conflict – see the
chart below. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91
unleaded was around 6% lower in September than in the
June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower.
Fuel spending accounts for around 4% of the CPI, and
these moves have taken 0.4ppts off inflation this quarter.
New Zealand petrol prices – 91 unleaded

While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in
the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully high. And
that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other
goods and services. Of note, the September quarter
saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with
international airfares up 14% and domestic airfares up

nearly 8%. Together those prices account for around 2%
of the CPI.
We also expect building costs (10% of the CPI) will rise by
1.6% this quarter following a similarly chunky rise in June.
Again, much of that rise has been related to higher global
oil prices which have been flowing through to higher
operating and material costs. That’s occurred at the same
time as the amount of planned construction work has
been rising.
The September quarter also sees the annual increases

in local council rates and charges for services like water
supply (around 4% of the CPI). We expect these costs will
be up around 7.4% this year, continuing the run of large
increases seen in previous years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices
(18% of the CPI), largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
the price for fresh vegetables.
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in
housing rents, which are the largest component of

the CPI, accounting for 10% of the basket. With low
population growth and ample supply, rents have
effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual
terms, rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more
than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Where could we be surprised?
There are some key areas where we see the risk
of a downside surprise. The first is the price for
communications equipment, like mobile phones. After
a large rise in the June quarter, we could see a sizeable

drop in prices this quarter.
There could also be softness in used car prices, especially
with high fuel prices encouraging a shift away from
combustion engine vehicles.
However, the risks aren’t all to one side. Demand for more
environmentally friendly new cars has picked up, and that
could push up prices for those vehicles. In addition, we’re
watching to see if high fuel costs are spilling over into
other prices as we’re seeing in sectors like construction.

Core inflation around 3%, not easing.
With big swings in fuel costs and concerns that this is
spilling over into a broader lift in inflation pressures,
the RBNZ will be paying close attention to the various
measures of core inflation (core inflation measures
smooth through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual
prices and instead track the underlying trend in inflation).
We expect that core inflation measures will point to
continued firmness in underlying price pressures, with

several closely watched measures likely to take a step
higher this quarter. In terms of specifics, we expect:
• CPI ex-fuel inflation: +3.0% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
• CPI ex-fuel and food: +3.2% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
• CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs
+2.5% previously)

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
We’ll also be watching the distribution of price
changes to see if there’s been a material increase in
the number of prices that are increasing. That could
be an early indication that businesses, even those
with a limited exposure to fuel costs, are starting to
factor in expectations of continued high inflation when
setting prices.
As we’ve noted before, the various measures of core
inflation were not low even before the recent fuel shock.

That means the RBNZ has limited headroom to absorb
upside inflation surprises.
Much of that firmness in core inflation has been due
to increases in domestically oriented non-tradables
prices. We estimate these prices rose by 1.3% over the
September quarter. That would see annual non-tradables
inflation rising to 3.6%, up from 3.4% last quarter.
Non-tradables inflation is a key focus for the RBNZ and
it’s been running at these above-average levels for around

a year now. That’s despite the softness in economic
growth and the labour market. In part, this firmness in
domestic inflation is due to continued large increases in
administered prices, like council rates.
On the imported front, lower fuel costs are expected to
see tradables prices falling by 0.2% over the quarter.
That would result in annual tradables inflation dropping
back to 3.9% from 4.9% previously. Excluding food and
fuel costs, we estimate that tradables prices were up

2.2% over the past year (vs 1.9% in the year to June). That
would be the highest level in three years with the fall in
the NZD boosting import costs.

Westpac
forecasts
How do our forecasts compare to the
RBNZ’s assumptions?
The RBNZ’s last published forecast from their September

policy statement assumed that consumer prices would
rise by 0.8%qtr (3.9%yr). That’s slightly higher than we’re
expecting. Most of the difference is because the RBNZ is
expecting higher imported inflation.
A result in line with our forecast would give the RBNZ a
little more breathing room ahead of their upcoming policy
review on 28 October. We’re expecting they will leave the
OCR on hold at this meeting, while markets are pricing in
a roughly 66% chance of a 25bp hike.

However, even if the overall inflation result is close to
forecast, the underlying detail will be important. The
RBNZ will be watching closely for signs that high fuel
prices are spilling over into other prices, especially with
global oil prices taking another step higher recently. With
that in mind, the strength of the various core inflation
measures will be a key focus for the RBNZ.
Furthermore, while annual inflation is expected to
soften in the September quarter, this is likely to be just a

temporary reprieve. With a sharp rise in fuel costs over
the past few weeks, inflation is set to reaccelerate back
over 4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain
above the RBNZ’s forecast for most of next year.
September monthly prices.
Our forecast for a 0.7% rise in consumer prices in the
September quarter incorporates information from
Stats NZ’s latest monthly prices update. That update
did reveal some softer than expected food price

movements in September. However, that was balanced
against larger than expected rises in airfares and holiday
accommodation costs.

## Document 367: The Commodities Feed_ Oil holds firm as Middle East breakthrough remains elusive

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Commodities Feed_ Oil holds firm as Middle East breakthrough remains elusive.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Oil is holding ﬁrm as Middle East tensions refuse to cool.
Meanwhile, IEA members agreed to release the rest of the
emergency stockpiles they pledged earlier this year

Warren Patterson and Ewa Manthey
Energy – IEA member to release remaining
oil
Oil remains well-supported. A pattern has set in: prices slide early in
the session, then claw back most of those losses by the close. As a
result, ICE Brent continues to comfortably trade above $100/bbl.
Uncertainty in the Middle East and concerns over the sustainability
of oil ﬂows through the Strait of Hormuz has the market pricing in a
signiﬁcant risk premium. The longer this pickup in ﬂows continues,
the more likely the market will start giving back some of this risk
premium. In order to see a full removal, there needs to be some kind
of agreement between the US and Iran. This would provide the
market with more conﬁdence that stronger oil ﬂows from the region
will continue.

### Page 2

International Energy Agency members agreed to release the
remaining 100m barrels of oil from strategic reserves. It's part of the
initial 400m barrels that members agreed back in March to help
with the Persian Gulf supply shortfall. According to the IEA,
members have distributed around 330m barrels to date. From the
100m barrels to be released, 20m barrels will be diesel, and will
make its way onto the market as soon as possible. The US probably
won’t be happy with the scale of the release, particularly regarding

diesel. Initial suggestions were that we could see around 50m
barrels of diesel released. The risk is that if the US believes these
volumes are not enough, the administration may mull restrictions
on US diesel exports once again.
The Financial Times reports that the US is considering easing
sanctions on Russia’s shadow tanker ﬂeet in bid to reduce fuel
costs. Tanker freight rates surged to record levels recently due to the
growing ineﬃciency in trade ﬂows out of the Persian Gulf. Since the

start of the Russia-Ukraine war, Russia has built a signiﬁcant ﬂeet of
tankers, which are largely sanctioned. The idea is that easing
sanctions on these vessels would free up tanker capacity for the
broader market. The key question is how eﬀective it might be in
bringing down spot tanker rates. It’s unclear how much of this
capacity is actually available. Given UK and EU sanctions, insurance
could still be an issue.
The latest data from the EIA shows that US commercial crude oil

inventories fell by 8.56m barrels over the last week, which is the
largest weekly decline since February. Hurricane activity in the Gulf
played part in the inventory decline, with oﬀshore production shut
in due to the storm. US output is estimated to have fallen by 300k
b/d week-on-week. Crude oil imports fell by 791k b/d WoW, a key
driver behind the fall in stocks. For reﬁned products, US reﬁneries
increased their utilisation rates by 0.8pp to 93.5%, leaving

utilisation rates at their highest seasonal levels in at least 20 years.
Despite this, distillate fuel stocks still fell by 2.53m barrels over the
last week. This leaves distillate inventories at record seasonal lows
as we head into winter. Meanwhile, gasoline stocks increased by
3.52m barrels.

### Page 3

Metals — Iron ore breaks below $90…
brieﬂy
Iron ore prices fell brieﬂy below $90/t yesterday, the lowest since
November 2022, as weak Chinese demand and abundant global
supply continued to weigh on sentiment. Prices have declined more
than 14% this year, pressured by the prolonged weakness in
China’s property and construction sectors. This is undermining
steel demand. China’s crude steel production during the ﬁrst eight
months of 2026 fell to its lowest level for the period since 2018,

highlighting the impact of the country’s real estate downturn. With
steel mills operating on thin margins, further production cuts
remain possible. Additional supply entering the market has kept
iron ore fundamentals well supplied. As a result, prices are expected
to remain rangebound, with near-term risks skewed to the
downside.
Steel inventories at major Chinese mills rose to 17mt in early
October, up 8.4% from late September, according to CISA data.

Inventories were 6.9% higher than a year earlier, indicating
subdued underlying demand. Meanwhile, crude steel production at
major mills increased 3.1% from late September to 1.94mt/d in
early October.
Agriculture —Cocoa demand recovery
support prices
Cocoa prices ﬁrmed yesterday, with London cocoa settling a little
more than 1% higher amid ﬁrmer demand numbers. Data from the
European Cocoa Association showed European cocoa grindings
rose 3.2% year-on-year and 10% quarter-on-quarter to 348.2kt in

3Q26, marking the ﬁrst annual increase since 2Q24. Similarly, the
Cocoa Association of Asia reported a 29.2% YoY increase in
grindings and a 4.1% QoQ rise to a record 233.8kt.
In its monthly update, the International Grains Council (IGC) raised
its 2026/27 global corn production forecast to 1,307mt from
1,301mt, while consumption was revised up to 1,323mt from
1,321mt. This saw global corn ending stocks increase to 297mt

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

## Document 422: 【國泰證期研究部】IC設計產業-智慧應用擴展，微控制器迎升級-20261019

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】IC設計產業-智慧應用擴展，微控制器迎升級-20261019.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 6

1

產業更新-IC 設計產業 
智慧應用擴展，微控制器迎升級 
What’s New –  Edge AI 與智慧控制，拓展 MCU 應用 
MCU角色正由家電、遙控器等基礎控制，延伸至語音喚醒、手勢與振動異常辨識，以及機器人
關節與靈巧手的精準控制。產品升級方向集中於三點：一是高效能 32-bit核心，Cortex-
M55/M85時脈拉高至 800MHz～1GHz；二是整合 AI加速器，ST STM32N6內建 Neural-ART 
NPU達 600 GOPS，新唐 M55M1與瑞薩 RA8P1採 Arm Ethos-U55；三是開發工具與參考設
計，如新唐 NuEdgeWise、瑞薩 RUHMI、TI CCStudio Edge AI Studio、雅特力 AT32 AI 
Studio。TI於 2026/3將 NPU下放至千顆單價低於 US$1的 Cortex-M0+產品，NPU已不再限
於高階 MCU。

Trends/Outlook –  傳統應用為基礎，新品升級添動能 
2Q26 大廠庫存已降至低檔，ST經銷商庫存低於標準目標、Microchip僅 25 天，MCU產業
正式脫離谷底。預期 2H26至 2027年補庫存延續，成熟製程、封測與記憶體成本上升支撐報
價，Infineon、TI、Microchip、瑞薩已陸續調價，盛群 8月起全產品線調漲 10～20%。需求
面以工控與資料中心最強，車用成長幅度低於工控，中國以舊換新補貼縮減，家電內需恐趨緩。
研究部預估 2026年全球 MCU市場成長逾 10%，中長期動能來自規格升級：整合 NPU的 AI 
MCU SRAM 達 1.5～4.2MB，遠高於傳統 MCU的數十至數百 KB；歐盟 CRA於 2027/12全
面適用，資安功能將成外銷產品必要規格。

Investment Summary 
1. MCU 成長來源由出貨量轉向出貨量＋規格；應用由基礎控制延伸至智慧感測、邊緣推
論與精準控制，成長同時來自終端補庫存需求，以及 32-bit、整合 NPU／馬達控制／資
安的規格升級。研究部預估 MCU端 AI於 2028年至少占全部 MCU的 10%，盛群 32-
bit ASP約為 8-bit的 2倍，升級對 ASP的拉抬效果明確。

2. 台廠主要受惠廠商包含新唐、盛群及雅特力-KY，海外則為 ST與瑞薩。ST的 STM32N6
已量產，瑞薩則有 RA8P1及 RA8T2機器人關節方案，兩家在 AI MCU 與機器人控制的
完整度領先；台廠中雅特力受惠中國工控與伺服器風扇等新應用， 1～9 月營收年增
121%，盛群 8月起漲價 10～20%且安防 MCU出貨創新高，新唐 BMC需求年增顯著，
日本廠受惠 8吋晶圓需求回升，轉盈為獲利關鍵。

2

控制功能廣泛，MCU 深入各類終端 
MCU 將處理器核心、記憶體與 ADC、PWM、通訊介面等周邊整合於單晶片，負責讀取訊號、
判斷並輸出控制，具低成本、低功耗與即時反應特性。 8-bit 定位於小家電、遙控器、電源管理
等簡單控制；32-bit涵蓋 Arm Cortex -M0+～M7、M33／M55／M85及 RISC-V，用於變頻馬
達、工控、車用與連網裝置，轉向 32-bit及高可靠 MCU為支撐 ASP的主因。 
全球 MCU市場由 2023年 US$28B降至 2024年 US$22.4B，年減約 20%，2025年再年減 0.3%，
仍處谷底； Infineon 以 23.2%市占居冠，前五大合計約 80%。研究部預估 2030 年市場逾
US$34B。

表 1. MCU 主要應用與產品需求比較 
類別 主要應用 位元／核心 升級方向 
家電 面板／觸控、變頻馬達、電源控制 8-bit～M4，變頻需 M4F 定頻轉變頻，一級能效規範帶動 32-bit FOC控制 
消費性 按鍵、充電、電池管理 8-bit～M0+／M4 快充與電池管理整合，要求低功耗、觸控及無線連網 
工控 伺服／馬達、PLC、數位電源 M4／M7，高階至 R52＋A55 伺服多軸控制、EtherCAT／TSN網路化及功能安全 
車用 車身、底盤、BMS、區域控制 RH850、AURIX等多核鎖步 區域控制架構整併 ECU，電動化帶動BMS、OBC與 ASIL D需求 
智慧終端 感測融合、連網、在地推論 M33／M55／M85＋NPU 語音與影像推論在地化，整合 NPU與資安認證 
資料來源：各公司資料、國泰證期研究部整理

終端需求分化，產品組合影響營運 
2Q26 MCU大廠一致宣告去庫存結束、轉入補庫存：ST Embedded Processing營收季增 17.7%
至 US$1,147M，經銷商庫存低於標準目標；Microchip經銷商庫存僅 25天；瑞薩預期 3Q26通
路將建庫存。價格面由中微 1月率先調漲 15～50%，新唐 6吋晶圓代工 7/1再漲 20～30%，成
本壓力由成熟製程擴及封測與記憶體。終端以工控與資料中心最強， ST 工業營收季增 20%，TI
工業與瑞薩工業、基礎設施及 IoT 事業皆季增約 10%；中國以舊換新補貼總額由 3,000 億降至
2,500億人民幣、補貼品類由 12項減為 6項，家電內需趨緩。 
兆易 1H26 MCU 營收年增 49%，陸廠正往工控、車用上移。通用型產品規格與價格透明，易陷
價格競爭；專用型產品則靠功能整合與服務建立差異，如盛群安防 MCU 受美國 UL 法規帶動，
2Q26出貨 1,965萬顆創新高。惟本波屬選擇性補庫存，成熟 MCU尚未全面缺貨，2027年需留
意重複下單後的修正風險；台廠轉單源於陸廠產能受限，產能回補後轉單可能回流。

### Page 3

投資 顧問 
產業報告

2026/10/19

3

邊緣智慧導入，MCU 整合 AI 功能 
AI MCU 在終端就地執行小型神經網路，用於語音喚醒、手勢、物件與振動異常偵測，資料不需
上傳雲端。架構分兩類：一類以 CPU、DSP或 Helium向量指令執行小型模型，適合語音與感測
等輕量應用；另一類整合專用 NPU，適合影像與較大模型。2026 年 NPU 由高階產品往低價與
車用兩端擴散：TI於 3月推出內建 TinyEngine NPU的 MSPM0G5187，千顆單價低於 US$1並
已量產，推論延遲較無 NPU產品最多降低 90倍、能耗降低逾 120倍；ST於 2月發表首款整合
NPU的車用 MCU Stellar P3E，NPU算力逾 80 GOPS，鎖定電動動力系統與 BMS，預計 4Q26
量產；NXP亦於 9月擴充 MCX N系列。高階產品如 ST STM32N6、瑞薩 RA8P1與新唐 M55M1
則已進入量產供貨，NPU算力約 110～600 GOPS。 
研究部預估 2031年 TinyML AI晶片出貨逾 41億顆，CAGR 37%，其中 NPU成長最快，CAGR
達 90%。

馬達控制升級，智慧設備擴展需求 
變頻與無刷馬達以 FOC閉迴路控制為主流，需含 FPU、DSP的 Cortex-M4／M7、高解析 PWM
與 Msps 級 ADC，需求正由家電、空調延伸至工業伺服、無人機與機器人關節。台廠中，新唐
KM1M7 系列具 208ps PWM 與 2Msps ADC；盛群 HT32F65432A／65440A 整合三相 gate 
driver主攻 BLDC；雅特力 AT32M416具 CAN-FD與磁編碼回授，鎖定人形與四足機器人。海
外瑞薩 RA8T2關節方案支援 EtherCAT並附演算法與原始碼，ST STM32G4為靈巧手常見中控。 
惟每台人形機器人約需 56顆 MCU，研究部預估 2026年中國出貨約 5萬台，需求約 280萬顆，
相較全球 MCU 年出貨逾 200億顆，占比不到 0.1%，短期量體仍以家電變頻、工業伺服與無人
機為主。

功能整合提高，軟體工具加速導入 
MCU 競爭已由晶片效能擴及感測、連網、資安與軟體工具。歐盟 CRA 自 2026/9/11 起要求製
造商於知悉遭利用漏洞後 24 小時內預警、72 小時內通報，2027/12/11 起全面適用，外銷歐盟
設備更重視 secure boot、TrustZone 與 PSA 認證；新唐 M55M1 具 PSA Level 2 ，瑞薩
RH850/U2C更導入 PQC加密。 
軟體方面，各廠將模型轉換與部署整合進開發平台：TI CCStudio Edge AI Studio內建 60+預訓
練模型，瑞薩 Renesas 365涵蓋設計、驗證至 OTA更新，新唐 NuEdgeWise提供模型訓練與部
署工具，馬達方案則如瑞薩 RA8T2 直接附演算法與原始碼。CRA 要求產品上市後持續修補漏洞，
MCU廠需提供長期資安更新，對研發資源有限的中小型廠商較不利。

### Page 4

投資 顧問 
產業報告

2026/10/19

4

表 2. 傳統 MCU 與智慧應用 MCU 比較 
項目 傳統 MCU 智慧應用 MCU 
功能 控制、感測讀取、通訊、簡單邏輯判斷 語音喚醒、手勢、物件偵測、振動異常偵測；多軸精準馬達控制 
效能需求 8-bit／M0+～M4，數十～200MHz；SRAM數十～數百 KB M33／M55／M85，150MHz～1GHz；NPU約 100～600 GOPS；SRAM 1.5～4.2MB 
整合方向 類比、計時器、基本通訊介面 NPU、ISP／MIPI CSI-2、DMIC、TSN／EtherCAT、TrustZone／PSA 
應用例子 家電面板、遙控器、電源管理、基本工控 智慧監控、穿戴／AR眼鏡、工業感測、機器人關節與靈巧手 
代表產品 盛群 8-bit系列、通用型 Cortex-M0+／M4 ST STM32N6／Stellar P3E、瑞薩 RA8P1、新唐 M55M1、TI MSPM0G5187 
資料來源：ST、Microchip、瑞薩、TI、NXP、新唐、兆易創新、盛群、國泰證期研究部整理

布局各異，智慧控制成共同方向 
在 AI MCU 方面，ST的 STM32N6已量產，切入邊緣視覺 AI；瑞薩 RA8P1以高效能核心搭配
NPU，布局語音與視覺應用。新唐 M55M1雖較早推出，但尚未見明確營收貢獻；盛群與雅特力
目前則以 CPU執行小型模型為主，雅特力另與聯電、智原合作開發 28nm eFlash邊緣 AI平台。
TI已將 NPU下放至 US$1以下產品，高階 NPU MCU後續將面臨價格下探壓力。 
在馬達控制與機器人方面，瑞薩 RA8T2 關節方案支援 EtherCAT，完整度最高；雅特力以
AT32M416切入機器人關節與靈巧手，新唐 KM1M7系列與盛群 BLDC MCU則以家電變頻及工
業馬達為主。由於人形機器人量體仍小，台廠的差異化轉向 AI 伺服器周邊：盛群布局散熱風扇
與光通訊 MCU，目標 2027年量產；雅特力推出伺服器風扇單晶片方案，新唐則以 BMC與 EC
為獲利主軸。

圖 1. 全球 MCU 市場規模

## Document 478: スペインは解散総選挙へ

Source PDF: all report pdf/スペインは解散総選挙へ.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日

MURC Focus

スペインは解散総選挙へ

～右派連立政権が成立する見込み～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 解散総選挙に臨んだサンチェス首相 
スペインのペドロ・サンチェス首相が 10 月 5 日、議会を解散し、11 月 29 日に総選挙（定数 350、比例代表制）
を行うと発表した。住宅価格や家賃の高騰に対する政府の緊急政令案が、10 月 2 日に下院で否決されたことを
受けて、少数与党による政権運営の行き詰まりを意識した首相が、国民に信を問う決断をした形である 。ただし、
サンチェス首相が率いる中道左派の与党・社会労働党（PSOE）の支持率は低迷している（図表 1）。

もともと PSOE の人気は低迷しており、前回 2023 年 7 月の総選挙でも、ライバル政党である中道右派の国民
党（PP）が第一党となっていた。しかしサンチェス首相は他の左派政党や地域政党の協力を受けて組閣し、政権
を維持した経緯がある。しかし首相周辺の汚職疑惑などから PSOE の人気は回復せず、次期の総選挙では PP
と同根の右派政党である Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。 
図表 1. 主要政党の支持率調査 図表 2. スペインの住宅価格

（出所）各種世論調査より

（出所）欧州連合統計局（ユーロスタット）

ユーロ圏 スペイン
（2015年=100、季調済）
（年、四半期）
〇スペインのサンチェス首相が10月5日、議会を解散し、11月29日に総選挙を行うと発表した。次期の総選挙
では野党第一党の PP と同根の右派政党 Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。 
〇PP と Vox が総選挙で勝利すれば、2018年6月以来の政権交代が実現する。こうした右派回帰を民族主義
の台頭という流れに位置付ける向きもあるが、スペインの国内事情はもっと複雑である。 
〇仮に総選挙を経て PP と Vox の連立政権が成立しても、両派間ですり合わせるべき課題も多く、スペイン政
治が安定するとは限らない。ゆえに、当面の問題への対応も進まないだろう。

### Page 2

解散の直接的な理由となった住宅価格の問題であるが、住宅市況が底にあった 2015 年を基準（＝100）とす
る指数でその動きを確認すると、直近2026 年4～6 月期は201 と、ユーロ圏全体（159）を大きく上回る（図表2）。
特に 2023 年以降の上昇が目覚ましいが、堅調な景気や移民の急増を受けて住宅需要が高まったことがその主
因である。

2. 民族主義的な右派回帰とは異なる 
PP と Vox が総選挙で勝利すれば、2018 年 6 月以来の政権交代が実現する。こうした右派回帰を民族主義
の台頭という流れに位置付ける向きもあるが、スペインの 国内事情は複雑であるため、必ずしもその流れに合わ
ない点、留意すべきである。まず、そもそも PSOE の人気そのものが低下している。確かに景気は堅調だが、住
宅価格の急騰のような社会問題に関して、サンチェス政権は具体的な処方箋を提示できなかった。

総選挙を経て政権交代が実現し、PP が Vox と連立を組んだとしても、それを民族主義の台頭の表れと理解す
るよりも、一定の期間ごとに繰り返される左派から右派へのリバランスの流れとして理解するほうが適切だろう。 ま
たスペインの場合、右派間に対立関係があることにも注目する必要がある。地域ナショナリズムが強いスペインで
は地方分権を求める保守の力が強く、中央集権を主張する保守（つまり Vox）と対立関係にある。

中央集権を望む保守の一部に戦前・戦後のフランコ体制を理想化する向きがあることも、地域ナショナリズムと
の対立を生んでいる。地域ナショナリズム政党の中には、自らを保守と位置付けながらも、中央集権を望む保守と
の対立から、PSOE や他の左派政党（Podemos や Sumar）と歩調を合わせる動きもある。スペインの民族主義は
多様なため、同国の右派回帰を反欧州連合（EU）や反移民の文脈で捉えることはできない。

例えばスペイン南東部のカタルーニャ州では、伝統的に左派も右派も民族主義を掲げ、独立運動（あるいは高
度な自治の獲得のための運動）を展開しているが、同時に票を奪い合う関係にある。最近は左派の カタルーニャ
共和主義左翼（ERC）が劣勢である。一方、右派のカタルーニャ同盟（AC）が優勢となっており、政党支持率では
PSOE に次ぐ第 2 位となっている。

AC は右派だが独立を主張しており、中央集権を主張する Vox とは対立する。一方、カタルーニャ州でも独立
に同調しない有権者は数多く、そうした有権者は PSOE に投票する傾向が強い。カタルーニャ州はスペインでも
有数の産業地帯であるため、労働運動が盛んだったことから、伝統的に PSOE の支持が厚いためである。このよ
うに、スペイン政治はまだら模様であるため、右派回帰と民族主義を同一視することは不適切である。

### Page 3

3. 総選挙後の新政権も多難な船出となる見込み 
総選挙後にいかなる立場の新政権が成立しようとも、スペイン経済の先行きに対する懸念は残る 。確かに景気
は堅調を維持しているが、一方、冒頭で述べた住宅価格の高騰のような社会問題に対する妙薬はなかなか存在
しない。加えて、経済成長の次なる牽引役を見いだせていないという問題もある。例えばスペイン景気を牽引する
輸出だが、旅行サービスに支えられたサービスの輸出は好調でもモノの輸出は不調である（図表3）。

中南米からの移民による労働供給が潤沢とはいえ、グローバルな労働力不足やスペインの人口構成（純増で
はあるが移民流入に依存した増加）に鑑みれば、スペインでも生産性の向上は不可欠である。それに資する総固
定資本形成は、実体として建設投資に偏っており、機械投資や知的財産投資の規模は限定的である（図表 4）。
これらの投資を増やすためには国による産業政策が欠かせない。

具体的には、規制緩和や税制優遇といった措置である。これまではサンチェス首相率いる PSOE 政権が少数
与党だったということや、PSOE が中道左派であるため経済成長よりも所得分配を重視したこともあり、そうした設
備投資を後押しするような政策が必ずしも打ち出されたわけではなかった。では PP と Vox の連立政権に交代し
たことでそれが可能になるかどうかというと、その可能性も高くない。

なぜならば、PP と Vox の連立政権が強いイニシアチブの下で産業政策を“上から”推し進めようとすると、中央
集権的な色彩が強くなり、地域ナショナリズムとの間であつれきが生じる展開が予想されるためである。中央集権
志向を弱めるという現実的な判断を Vox ができるかどうかが鍵を握るが、同党にはもともと PP から右派色の濃い
グループが離脱して結党した経緯があるだけに、その余地がどの程度か定かではない。

現状では総選挙を経て PSOE から PP と Vox の連立へと政権が交代する公算が大きいが、両党ですり合わせ
るべき課題も多く、政治が安定するとは限らない。ゆえに、当面の問題への対応も進まないだろう。 
図表 3. GDP に占める輸出の割合 図表 4. GDP に占める固定資本形成の割合

## Document 482: ニュージーランド総選挙は新興政党がカギを握る、NZドル相場は？ ～政権交代観測とRBNZのマンデート変更がNZドルの重しに～

Source PDF: all report pdf/ニュージーランド総選挙は新興政党がカギを握る、NZドル相場は？ ～政権交代観測とRBNZのマンデート変更がNZドルの重しに～.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 10 月 1 日

1/2 
ニュージーランド総選挙は新興政党がカギを握る、NZ ドル相場は？ 
～政権交代観測と RBNZ のマンデート変更が NZ ドルの重しに～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランドでは 10 月１日に議会が解散し、11 月７日の総選挙に向けた選挙戦が本格
化した。 二大政党の支持率低下が続くなか、 土地税の導入と年間1.94 万ＮＺドルのベーシッ
クインカムに相当する 「市民所得」 の支給を掲げる新興のオポチュニティ党が支持を伸ばし、
初の議席獲得と連立政権の行方を左右する可能性が浮上している。 世論調査では、 最大野党 ・
労働党を中心とする左派陣営が同党の支持を得れば政権を樹立し得る一方、現与党連合が過
半数を確保できるかも不透明である。 
○ 金融市場では、政権交代時にRBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）の政策目標へ「持続可能な
雇用の最大化」 が再び加えられる可能性が意識され、 ＮＺドルは対米ドルで10 ヵ月ぶりの安
値圏に弱含んでいる。足元の失業率が10 年ぶりの高水準となるなか、 マンデート変更が実現
すれば追加利上げのハードルが高まるとの見方もあり、総選挙後の連立協議と金融政策への
影響がＮＺドル相場の先行きを左右する可能性がある。

ニュージーランド議会（代議院：一院制（総議席数120） ）が10 月１日に解散し、11 月７日に実
施予定の総選挙に向けた動きが正式に始まった。 同国では、30 年前にドイツ型の小選挙区比例代表
併用制（ＭＭＰ）が導入されて以来、右派の国民党、ないし左派の労働党を中心とする連立政権が
一般的となっている。さらに、このところは二大政党の支持率の低下傾向に歯止めがかからず、多
数政党との連立による政権樹立を余儀なくされる展開が続いている。 
こうしたなか、今回の総選挙では、新興政党・オポチュニティ党が「台風の目」となる可能性が
高まっている。同党は結党から 10 年を迎えるものの、過去の総選挙では議席獲得に必要な得票率
基準（５％）を満たすことができなかった。しかし、最新の世論調査では、支持率が基準を上回っ
て初めて議席を獲得し、キャスティングボートを握る可能性が高まっている。背景には、同党が掲
げる公約が影響している。同党は、都市部で年間1.75％、農村部で0.5％の土地税を新設し、それ
を財源の一部として、 原則18 歳以上の国民などに年間 1.94 万ＮＺドルのベーシックインカム （Ｂ
Ｉ）に相当する「市民所得」を支給する税制改正を公約に掲げる。同党はその目的について、国民
生活の下支えに加え、 高止まりする住宅価格の抑制につながるとの見方を示す（図１） 。さらに、 同

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
党は、 土地税導入に伴い住宅価格は下落
するものの、 ＢＩの支給に伴い国民の９
割以上が現状と同等、 ないし上回る生活
水準を維持可能との独自のシミュレー
ションを公表している。なお、国民党、
労働党のいずれの党首も税制改正案の
採用を否定するなど、 実現のハードルは
高い。一方、 同党の政策には左派陣営と
親和性のあるものもみられることから、
選挙後の連立協議の行方が注目される。

最新の世論調査によれば、最大野党・労働党を中心とする左派陣営が、オポチュニティ党の支持
を得れば政権を樹立し得る情勢が示されている。一方、国民党、ＡＣＴ党、ニュージーランド・フ
ァースト党で構成される現与党連合につ
いても、単独で過半数を確保できるかは
不透明となっているこのところの金融市
場においては、通貨ＮＺドルの対米ドル
相場が 10 ヵ月ぶりの安値水準で推移す
るなど弱含んでいる（図２） 。背景には、
RBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）の政
策運営が、政治的な争点となっているこ
とがある。 労働党などはRBNZ のマンデー
ト（政策目標）の変更を公約に掲げてお
り、政権交代となれば、持続可能な雇用
の最大化が再び加えられると見込まれる。足元の雇用環境を巡っては、直近４-６月の失業率が 10
年ぶりの高水準となったことが確認されている。RBNZ は９月の定例会合で追加利上げを決定した
ものの （注１）、仮に政権交代によってマンデートが変更されれば、 先行きの政策運営を巡って追加
利上げのハードルが高まるとの見方につながる可能性がある。こうした事情がＮＺドル相場の重し
になっているとみられる。足元のオセアニア通貨は、豪ドル相場も下値を探る動きをみせ るなか、
ＮＺドルを巡る状況にも注意が必要となっている。 
以 上

  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/665879.html）

## Document 487: フランス予算審議、極右がキングメーカーに浮上 ～大統領選を前に崩れる社会党との協力関係～

Source PDF: all report pdf/フランス予算審議、極右がキングメーカーに浮上 ～大統領選を前に崩れる社会党との協力関係～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Europe Trends

2026 年 10 月２日

1/3 
フランス予算審議、極右がキングメーカーに浮上 
～大統領選を前に崩れる社会党との協力関係～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ フランス政府は１日に閣議決定した 2027 年予算案で、財政再建を軌道に戻すため、新たに
430 億ユーロの財政改善措置を盛り込んだ。だが、少数与党のルコルニュ政権には予算を成
立させる議会基盤がなく、前年に予算成立で協力した穏健左派政党の社会党は、大統領選を
前に政権との対決姿勢を強めている。代わって予算成立の鍵を握りつつあるのが、大統領選
挙での勝利を目指す極右政党の国民連合（ＲＮ）だ。今後の焦点は、年金抑制や増税・歳出
削減を巡る修正を通じ、社会党またはＲＮの不信任回避を取り付けられるかに移る。

■430 億ユーロの追加財政措置を盛り込んだ政府予算案 
フランス政府は10 月１日、2027 年予算案を閣議決定した。2026 年に国内総生産（ＧＤＰ）比
で 5.4％程度に膨らむ見通しの財政赤字を、2027 年に 5.0％へ縮小することを目指す。財政再建を
軌道に戻すには540 億ユーロの財政改善措置が必要とされ、既に決定済みの措置に加えて、2027
年予算案で新たに430 億ユーロを盛り込んだ。このうちの約６割は歳出抑制を通じて捻出すると
しており、公務員給与の算定基準の凍結、国防など一部の優先分野を除く省庁予算の削減、地方
政府への歳出削減要求、年金給付の物価連動の一部停止、医療費の伸び抑制、疾病手当や労災給
付の厳格化などを実施する。歳入面では、年金受給者向け所得控除の縮小、企業向け社会保険料
負担軽減措置の縮小、糖分含有量の多い加工食品への課税強化などを盛り込んだ。家計や企業を
対象とした一律の大型増税は見送ったが、税・社会保険料負担のＧＤＰ比は現在の43.9％から
2027 年に44.2％へ上昇する見通しだ。

■前年の予算成立を支えた社会党は態度を硬化 
予算審議は難航が避けられない。９月 24 日付けレポート「難航するフランスの予算協議」で指
摘した通り、ルコルニュ首相が率いる政府は国民議会（下院）で過半数を持たず、予算成立には
野党の協力が必要となる。憲法 49 条３項に基づく手続きを用いれば、議会で予算法案そのものを
採決することなく成立させることができるが、野党は内閣不信任案を提出できる。不信任案が否
決されれば法案は採択されたものと見做される一方、可決されれば内閣は総辞職する。 
前年の予算協議では、予算未成立のまま越年した後、穏健左派の野党・社会党（ＰＳ）の協力

### Page 2

2/3 
で予算成立に漕ぎ着けた。社会党は、マクロン大統領の目玉政策である年金改革の停止などを条
件に、49 条３項発動後の内閣不信任案への賛成を見送り、予算成立と政権存続を容認した。今回
発表された予算案には、社会党が求める富裕層や高収益企業への課税強化などが盛り込まれなか
った一方で、年金や社会保障を中心に幅広い負担を求める措置が含まれている。社会党のフォー
ル第一書記は政府の予算案について、 「何も変わらなければ不信任にする」と警告している。2027
年春の大統領選挙が近づくなか、社会党が再び予算成立と政権存続に協力する政治的なハードル
は前年より高まっている。政府が再び社会党の不信任回避を取り付けるには、予算案の大幅な修
正が必要になる。

■予算成立のキングメーカーに浮上する国民連合 
こうしたなか、予算成立の鍵を握りつつあるのが、大統領選挙での勝利を目指す極右政党・国
民連合（ＲＮ）だ。ＲＮの大統領候補であるルペン氏は、年金の物価連動を抑制する措置などに
反対しており、今回の政府案に反対票を投じる可能性を示唆している。同時に、 「不完全な予算で
あっても、予算がないよりマシ」との趣旨の発言を示唆しており、予算成立そのものを阻止しな
い可能性も示唆している。政府が49 条３項を発動した場合でも、他の野党が提出する内閣不信任
案への賛成を見送る可能性がある。 
ＲＮは財政再建そのものを否定している訳ではなく、年金受給者や現役世代への負担増に反対
する代わりに、政府機関の経費削減、外国人向け給付の削減、欧州連合（ＥＵ）への拠出金削減
などを要求している。政府案には、外国人が一部の社会給付を受給する際の居住要件を厳しくす
る措置など、ＲＮの主張と重なるものも含まれる。政府側はＲＮへの譲歩との見方を否定してい
るものの、ＲＮが予算成立を左右する可能性が高まっている。

■不信任回避に向けた与野党協議が本格化 
予算案は10 月中旬から国民議会の本会議で本格的な審議に入る。例年の審議日程を考えると、
まずは歳入部分を審議し、その後に歳出部分や社会保障予算の審議が続き、11 月中旬にかけて国
民議会での審議が進められ、年内の成立を目指すことになる。この１ヶ月余りが、ルコルニュ政
権にとって野党との妥協点を探る重要な期間となる。まずは議会審議を通じて野党の修正案を部
分的に取り込み、そのうえで通常採決を目指すのか、49 条３項を用いるかを判断することになる
だろう。予算案そのものへの積極的な賛成を取り付けることが難しい場合も、社会党かＲＮの少
なくとも一方が内閣不信任に加わらない内容まで予算案を修正できるかどうかが重要となる。 
焦点の１つが年金給付の抑制だ。政府案では、低額年金を物価連動停止の対象外とする一方、
月額 1260 ユーロを上回る年金については物価連動を停止する。年金抑制を緩和する代わりに富裕
層や大企業への課税を強化する方向であれば社会党との距離を縮めやすくなり、歳出削減を上積
みし、外国人向け給付などを厳格化する方向であればＲＮの要求に近づく。両党が求める修正の
方向性は大きく異なり、政府は限られた財政余地のなかで難しい選択を迫られる。

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
■予算審議が映す大統領選前の政治力学 
政府にはもう１つの非常手段も残されている。憲法47 条では、議会が予算案の提出から70 日
以内に審議を終えなければ、政府は予算案の規定を政令（オルドナンス）によって実施すること
が認められている。今回の場合、12 月17 日がその期限となる。現在の政治体制（第五共和制）の
下でこの手法が実際に使われた例はなく、実施すれば大きな政治的な反発を招く可能性がある。 
2027 年予算を巡る攻防は、単なる財政再建策の調整にとどまらない。大統領選挙を目前に控
え、各党が政権との距離の取り方を模索するなか、前年まで予算成立を支えた社会党との協力関
係は弱まり、代わってＲＮの対応が政権存続を左右する可能性が増している。ルコルニュ政権に
とって残された選択肢は、社会党かＲＮのどちらかに全面的に接近することではなく、双方との
交渉を続けながら、少なくとも一方が不信任に踏み切らないぎりぎりの妥協点を探ることにな
る。予算審議の行方は、大統領選挙を前にフランス政治の重心がどこへ移りつつあるのかを映し
出すことにもなりそうだ。

以 上

## Document 515: 中国の貿易統計（26年9月）～輸出入とも前月に続き加速、半導体関連は高い伸びを維持

Source PDF: all report pdf/中国の貿易統計（26年9月）～輸出入とも前月に続き加速、半導体関連は高い伸びを維持.pdf

### Page 1

１．概況 
・中国海関総署が 2026 年 10 月 14 日に 26 年 9 月の貿易統計の速報値を発表した。財輸出（ドル建
て、図表 1）は前年同月比＋36.3％となった （前月は同＋25％）。財輸入は同＋31％となった （前月
は同＋28.2％）。貿易収支は＋1,335 億ドルと、前年同月（＋904 億ドル）から増加した。当社試算
の季節調整値でみると、輸出は前月比＋10.2％、輸入は同＋7.2％であった。 
・財輸出（人民元建て、図表 2）は前年同月比＋29.6％となった（前月は同＋18.6％）。財輸入は同＋
24.6％となった（前月は同＋21.7％） 。貿易収支は＋9,060 億元と、前年同月（＋6,455 億元）から
増加した。

２．詳細 
（１）輸出 
・国・地域別（ドル建て、前年同月比）では、米国向けで増加が続いており、前月に続き加速した。
ASEAN 向け、日本向け、EU 向けは、いずれも加速した（図表 3）。 
・品目別（同上）では、鋼材や携帯電話が減速した。プラスチック製品や紡績糸等、衣類等、コンピ
ュータ・同部品、家電、自動車等は加速した。集積回路も加速し、3 桁の伸びが続いている（図表
4）。

## Document 559: 内外経済ウォッチ『欧州～フランスの財政再建を阻む政治の壁～』(2026年11月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/内外経済ウォッチ『欧州～フランスの財政再建を阻む政治の壁～』(2026年11月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

欧州
～フランスの財政再建を阻む政治の壁～
フランスの財政不安が広がっている。 10月に発表した
2027年予算案では、 2026年に国内総生産 （Ｇ Ｄ Ｐ ） 比
5.4％に達する見込みの財政赤字を、 2027年に5.0％へ
縮小する方針を掲げた。 政府はこう した計画を達成する
ため、 年金の物価スライ ドの部分停止や税制優遇の縮小
を盛り込んだが、 年金受給者に負担を求める財政再建策
に野党勢が猛反発しており、 予算協議が難航している。
2024年の国民議会 （下院） 選挙以降、 安定多数を持
つ政治勢力がなく、 政府は予算協議のたびに野党との
妥協を迫られてきた。 昨年は年内に予算を成立できず、
年明け後に年金改革の棚上げを条件に野党の協力を取
り付けた。 一昨年は予算協議の難航で政権が崩壊した。
政治の混乱と財政再建の遅れへの警戒から、 フランスの
10年物国債利回りは2002年以来の高水準に上昇して
いる。
本格的な財政再建には年金や医療、 地方財政、 公務員
給与などへの切り込みが避けられない。 だが、 年金給付

や医療費の抑制には国民や労働組合が反発し、 地方への
支出削減には自治体が抵抗する。 フランスは既に税 ・ 社
会保険料負担が重く、 増税にも限界がある。 企業増税は
競争力の低下を招き、 富裕層課税で巨額の赤字を埋める
ことも難しい。 財政再建には幅広い層への負担を伴う
が、 現在の政治環境では合意形成が極めて難しい。
進まない財政再建、 予算協議は難航
財政再建をさらに難し く しているのが、 2027年春に
迫る大統領選挙だ。 選挙を前に、 与野党とも有権者に痛
みを求める政策を支持しにくい。 大統領選挙の世論調査
は、 極右政党 ・ 国民連合 （ Ｒ Ｎ） のマリーヌ ・ ルペン氏が圧
倒的にリー ド している。 Ｒ Ｎは歳出削減を通じて財政再建
に取り組む方針を示唆しているが、 その具体策には欧州
連合 （ Ｅ Ｕ） への拠出金削減など、 実効性が疑われる内容
も含まれる。 大統領選後の財政再建の行方や欧州連合
（ Ｅ Ｕ） との関係悪化も不安視される。
フランスの財政不安が他の欧州諸国に広がり、 2010

年代前半の欧州債務危機のような深刻な危機に発展す
る可能性は今のところ低い。 フランスはユーロ圏有数の
経済規模と厚みのある国債市場を持ち、 市場での資金
調達から締め出されているわけではない。 債務危機後
の欧州は、 金融安全網の整備や危機の再発防止策など
を整備してきた。
フランスの苦境は、 日本にと っても対岸の火事ではな
い。 日本も高齢化に伴う社会保障費の増加と巨額の政
府債務を抱え、 金利上昇が続けば利払い費が膨らむ。 両
国の事情は異なるが、 財政再建の遅れが将来の政策余
力を狭め、 市場の信任を損ないかねない点は共通する。
日本にと っても、 財政健全化と政治的合意形成をどう両
立するかは避けて通れない課題だ。
大統領選挙で高まる不透明感
経済調査部　首席エコノミス ト　田中 理（たなか おさむ）
資料1 フランスの財政収支の対Ｇ Ｄ Ｐ 比率
（注） 2026年は見込み、 2027年は政府予算案の計画
（出所） フランス財務省資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成
資料2 フランス大統領選挙の世論調査 （初回投票）

（注） 調査期間は2026年9月22～24日
（出所） Harris Interactive資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成

## Document 616: 多资产周报：通胀数据偏鹰，美债收益率仍下行

Source PDF: all report pdf/多资产周报：通胀数据偏鹰，美债收益率仍下行.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

S0980513100001
基础数据
固定资产投资累计同比 -7.20
社零总额当月同比 0.40
出口当月同比 36.30
M2 7.20
市场走势
资料来源：Wind、国信证券经济研究所整理
相关研究报告
《多资产周报-实际利率高企下的黄金反弹》 ——2026-10-11
《宏观经济周报-地产对冲政策试水》 ——2026-10-04
《多资产周报-全球股市显著分化》 ——2026-10-04
《多资产周报-加息落地，黄金与纳指背道而驰》 ——
2026-09-27
《宏观经济周报-政策资金加速落地》 ——2026-09-27
通胀数据偏鹰，美债收益率仍下行。本周美国通胀数据超出预期，本应推
高利率，但美债 10 年期由 5.2%上方下行约 15 个基点收于 5.098%。当
前美债买盘可归四类。一是避险需求。市场更重视增长与风险偏好收缩，
避险资金压过了数据过热的机械映射。二是政策沟通压短端。威廉姆斯
称不急于连续行动，沃什在 IMF不做预判，10月加息概率一度冲至 31.6%
后回落至约 20%。三是供给技术面。财政部再度回购，减轻私人部门吸

收久期的压力，30 年期与 10 年期同步回落，曲线牛平。这一条易误读
为财政转向，实际只是节奏管理。四是仓位与凸性，最被低估。测算显
示债券基金久期已显著低配，此后 3 至 6 个月收益率偏下行；抵押贷款
提前还款风险下降与最便宜可交割券切换迫使对冲者在上行时卖出久
期，仅此一项即需卖出约 600 亿美元 10 年等价，资管机构另需 100 至
150亿。趋势跟踪型基金的久期空头同样拥挤，回补需 10 年期降至 4.85%
至 4.95%。但科技股的定价锚已换到分子端与融资端。往后到 11 月初有
两个观测点。一是 10月 28日联邦公开市场委员会声明与点阵，当前基
准情形是 10 月不动、12 月加一次，关键看 10月加息概率能否守住 20%；
若回升至 30%至 40%，本周的牛平将被重读为流动性交易。二是 11 月 3
日中期选举结果对财政预期的定价。若指向财政扩张受阻，供给对长端
的压制减轻，本周的战术交易才有机会升级为趋势变化。

下跌 3.4%，恒生指数下跌 0.11%，标普 500 下跌 1.95%；债券方面，中
债10年下跌3.7BP，美债10年下跌15BP；汇率方面，美元指数下跌0.44%，
离岸人民币升值 0.11%；大宗商品方面，SHFE螺纹钢下跌 0.14%，伦敦
金现上涨 0.07%，伦敦银现下跌 4.15%，LME铜下跌 0.64%，LME铝下跌
1.36%，WTI原油上涨 10.41%。

值为 143.93，较上周下降 16.01；铜螺比数值为 35.49，较上周下降 0.15；
金铜比数值为 0.29，较上周上升 0.01；股债性价比数值为 4.74，较上
周上升 0.26；AH 股溢价数值为 128.56，较上周上升 2.5。

### Page 4

周度观察：通胀数据偏鹰，美债收益率仍下行
本周美国通胀数据超出预期，本应推高利率，但美债全周牛市趋平，10年期由 5.2%
上方下行约 15 个基点收于 5.098%，为本年 2 月以来最佳单周。同期纳指周跌
4.48%，费半八连跌为 2008 年以来最长。数据偏鹰而利率偏多，是本周最需要解
释的一组背离。
当前美债买盘可归四类。一是避险需求。市场更重视增长与风险偏好收缩，避险
资金压过了数据过热的机械映射。二是政策沟通压短端。威廉姆斯称不急于连续
行动，沃什在 IMF 不做预判，10 月加息概率一度冲至 31.6%后回落至约 20%。三
是供给技术面。财政部再度回购，减轻私人部门吸收久期的压力，30 年期与 10
年期同步回落，曲线牛平。这一条易误读为财政转向，实际只是节奏管理。四是
仓位与凸性，最被低估。测算显示债券基金久期已显著低配，此后 3 至 6 个月收
益率偏下行；抵押贷款提前还款风险下降与最便宜可交割券切换迫使对冲者在上
行时卖出久期，仅此一项即需卖出约 600 亿美元 10 年等价，资管机构另需 100

至 150 亿。趋势跟踪型基金的久期空头同样拥挤，回补需 10 年期降至 4.85%至
4.95%。
但科技股的定价锚已换到分子端与融资端。第一层是收入斜率。Anthropic 三季
度营收 168 亿美元，环比增约 45%，二季度为 147%，按 7 月底年化收入 650亿反
推，8、9两月月环比仅 3%至 4%。减速不构成需求转弱，却打掉了 AI 收入无限加
速的假设。第二层是融资端重定价，这是与 7 月回调的本质区别。CoreWeave 2032
年无担保债最差收益率破 16%，5 年期信用违约互换隐含违约概率升至约 61%，6
月为 31%，并外溢至甲骨文 263个基点。
往后到 11月初有两个观测点。一是 10 月 28 日联邦公开市场委员会声明与点阵，
当前基准情形是 10 月不动、12 月加一次，关键看 10月加息概率能否守住 20%；
若回升至 30%至 40%，本周的牛平将被重读为流动性交易。二是 11 月 3日中期选
举结果对财政预期的定价。若指向财政扩张受阻，供给对长端的压制减轻，本周

的战术交易才有机会升级为趋势变化。
多资产图景
1、整体收益率
本周（10月 10 日至 10 月 17 日），权益方面，沪深 300 下跌 3.4%，恒生指数下
跌 0.11%，标普 500 下跌 1.95%；债券方面，中债 10 年下跌 3.7BP，美债 10年下
跌 15BP；汇率方面，美元指数下跌 0.44%，离岸人民币升值 0.11%；大宗商品方
面，SHFE 螺纹钢下跌 0.14%，伦敦金现上涨 0.07%，伦敦银现下跌 4.15%，LME 铜
下跌 0.64%，LME 铝下跌 1.36%，WTI原油上涨 10.41%。

报告中投资建议所涉及的评级（如有）分为股票评
级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报
告发布日后6到 12个月内的相对市场表现，也即报
告发布日后的6到12个月内公司股价（或行业指数）
相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅作为基
准。A股市场以沪深 300指数（000300.SH）作为基
准；新三板市场以三板成指（899001.CSI）为基准；
香港市场以恒生指数(HSI.HI)作为基准；美国市场
以标普 500指数(SPX.GI)或纳斯达克指数
（IXIC.GI）为基准。
股票
投资评级
优于大市 股价表现优于市场代表性指数 10%以上
中性 股价表现介于市场代表性指数±10%之间
弱于大市 股价表现弱于市场代表性指数 10%以上
无评级 股价与市场代表性指数相比无明确观点
行业
投资评级
优于大市 行业指数表现优于市场代表性指数 10%以上
中性 行业指数表现介于市场代表性指数±10%之间
弱于大市 行业指数表现弱于市场代表性指数 10%以上

## Document 630: 宏观点评：三重拐点的信号初现——三季度和9月经济数据点评

Source PDF: all report pdf/宏观点评：三重拐点的信号初现——三季度和9月经济数据点评.pdf
Report source: 东吴证券
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告·宏观报告·宏观点评

东吴证券研究所

2026 年 10 月 19 日

证券分析师  芦哲 
执业证书：S0600524110003 
 luzhe@dwzq.com.cn 
证券分析师  占烁 
执业证书：S0600524120005 
 zhansh@dwzq.com.cn
